# Pine Forensic Audit (Phase 2)

Method: every script in `research/pine/` was read in full by a dedicated
audit agent against a fixed semantic checklist (request.security semantics,
barstate discipline, repainting, session/timezone, persistent state, order
semantics, alert discipline, executability, translation feasibility), with
findings returned in a structured schema and compiled by
`scripts/build_audit_docs.py`. Machine-readable findings:
`research/pine_findings.json`. Compilation on TradingView could NOT be
validated from this environment; compile status is `UNVERIFIED` for all
scripts (ASSUMPTIONS A-02).

Status meanings follow the build brief. `NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR` is not a
defect: it marks tools with no trading rules.

## Summary

| Status | Count |
|---|---|
| `NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR` | 28 |
| `PASS_WITH_CONSTRAINTS` | 13 |
| `REQUIRES_REWRITE` | 1 |

## Per-script findings

### 00 · Five-Tool Confluence AIO v3.6

- File: `research/pine/00_five_tool_confluence_aio.pine` · SHA-256 `e51d5a40d2e933bf…` · 2444 lines · Pine v6 · 219 inputs
- Classification: **strategy** · Family: **regime_detection** · Direction: bidirectional · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`PASS_WITH_CONSTRAINTS`**
- Explanation: The script's own decision logic is non-repainting and lookahead-safe: both HTF security calls use the standard safe idiom expr[1] + lookahead_on (lines 772-775), the benchmark call is same-TF with lookahead_off (743-744), every entry requires barstate.isconfirmed (confirmed at 855, enforced in can_long 1466-1469 and can_short 1470-1474), the regime state machine only commits on confirmed bars (604-605), divergence uses properly offset pivots osc[piv_r]/low[piv_r]/high[piv_r] (802-823), and there is no varip. It is a complete strategy (entries, protective stops at order time 1588/1631, targets, trail, sizing, commissions/slippage declared 29-31). Constraints preventing a clean PASS: (a) use_bar_magnifier=true (line 36) makes same-bar T1-limit-vs-stop resolution depend on TradingView intrabar data — historical fills are not reproducible from chart-TF OHLCV alone; (b) the SHORT+/LONG+ Markov gates (1134-1147, 1412-1425) and dwell percentiles feed on transition counts accumulated from the first loaded bar, so entry decisions depend on how much chart history TradingView loads (max_bars_back 5000, line 37) — deterministic only if the data start is pinned; (c) sizing and halts read strategy.equity (895-935), a fill-model-dependent feedback loop; (d) the optional external regime override via input.source (135, 138) imports unauditable third-party series (validation at 626-639 checks value domain, not repaint). None of these are lookahead contamination; they are reproducibility/translation constraints.
- Timeframes: Designed for swing timeframes with auto-preset mapping (lines 523-536): chart TF <= 4H uses the "4H" preset, >= 1W uses "Weekly", otherwise "Daily". Optional intraday session filters (lines 243-244, 297-298, default OFF) show intraday use is anticipated but not primary. Daily-loss halt only arms on charts <= 1D (line 900). HTF filter (default "D", lines 164-167) self-disables unless the higher TF exceeds the chart TF (lines 770-771).
- Symbols: US equities/ETFs implied: default benchmark AMEX:SPY (line 154), dollar-volume liquidity filter (lines 345-347, 889-893), session default "0935-1530". Degrades gracefully for no-volume symbols via equal-weight AVWAP option (line 181) and MFI volume-dead detection (line 785). Point-value-aware sizing (lines 317, 414-415) extends to futures. Exchange-timezone dependence in time("D") and session windows assumes a symbol with sane exchange sessions.
- External data: Three request.security calls: (1) line 743-744 bench_close = security(bench_sym, timeframe.period, close, gaps_off, lookahead_off) — same-TF cross-symbol benchmark for Mansfield RS and benchmark EMA filter; (2) lines 772-773 htf_close = security(tickerid, htf_req_tf, close[1], lookahead_on); (3) lines 774-775 htf_ema = security(tickerid, htf_req_tf, ta.ema(close, htf_ema_len)[1], lookahead_on). Also optional input.source external regime/strength links (lines 135, 138) that read another indicator's plot — external data whose integrity cannot be audited from this script.
- Entries: Two selectable systems per side, all requiring confirmed bar, in-date-window, matching regime (+1 long / -1 short), flat position, entry gates (quality/strength/stability/gap/overextension, 726-735), env filters (benchmark, HTF, ATR%, liquidity, AVWAP-alive, risk halts, cooldown, 1018-1022) and score >= min_score (default 55). Legacy long (856-880): regime flip, hidden divergence above AVWAP, regular divergence in value zone, or AVWAP reclaim with RS leadership, scored 0-100. Long v2 (1284-1462, default OFF): leader pullback / bull-flip first retest / AVWAP reclaim / tight-base breakout, minus no-chase, resistance-room, gap-exhaustion blocks, plus optional LONG+ Markov-stickiness/session/range/close-location/volume gate. Short v2 (1024-1280, default ON but allow_shorts default OFF): failed AVWAP reclaim with rejection candle, bear-flag breakdown, or SHORT+ bear-flip first retest, requiring relative weakness, minus no-chase/support-room/squeeze blocks, plus SHORT+ premium gate (Markov bear stay-probability with Raw/Wilson/Laplace estimator, regime youth by age/maturity/dwell percentile, continuation score >= 72, range/close-location/volume). Entries fill next bar open (process_orders_on_close=false).
- Exits: Three-leg split (f_reset_plan, 1542-1551). At entry: protective stop PROT_* at close -/+ stop distance placed with the orders (1588-1594, 1631-1637); once position is on, PROTs are cancelled and replaced (1667-1674) with: L1/S1 limit at T1 (t1_r x R) + leg stop, L2/S2 limit at T2 + leg stop, L3/S3 runner on run_stop. Breakeven (+optional offset R) after T1 detected via leg-1 no longer open (1651-1658). Runner: monotonic chandelier trail (ch_len/ch_mult) activated after trail_after_r in profit (1662-1666). Discretionary confirmed-bar closes: regime against position (1695-1704), short AVWAP reclaim (1705-1708), long AVWAP failure (1709-1712), long RS deterioration (1713-1717, default OFF), base-pivot failure (1718-1722, default OFF), max-bars time stop (1723-1732, default OFF). Stops are structural (beyond supply shelf/support shelf +/- ATR buffer, 1090-1092, 1346-1348) when enabled, else ATR multiple.
- Sizing: Risk-based: qty = (equity x risk_pct x risk_scale [x SHORT+ conviction mult]) / (stop_distance x point_value), capped by notional cap_pct of equity, floored to qty_step, rejected below min_qty (f_plan_qty/f_plan_qty_mult, 1522-1541). risk_scale = chop factor (1.0/0.75/0.45) x mature-extension factor (0.70) (737-739). SHORT+ multiplier: 1.20 A-grade / 1.00 standard / 0.00 blocked (1247-1251). Optional per-side virtual capital split sizes each side off its own sleeve equity ledger (308-309, 915-935, 1554, 1597). Position split into thirds when qty >= 3x min_qty. Pyramiding=3 exists solely to admit the 3 simultaneous legs; re-entry is prevented by the position_size==0 requirement. Commission 0.03% + 2 ticks slippage declared (29-31).
- Alerts: Dual mechanism: three static alertcondition()s (2395-2397: long setup, short setup, regime flip) plus dynamic alert() calls gated by can_long/can_short/regime_flip at alert.freq_once_per_bar_close (2398-2423), optionally emitting JSON payloads for webhook automation (use_json_alerts, 2401-2403, 2411-2413). Confirmed-bar discipline is solid: can_long/can_short embed barstate.isconfirmed and the flip alert is explicitly isconfirmed-gated (2421). Duplicate-fire risk: a user enabling both the alertcondition and the script-generated alert() receives two notifications per signal; also a persisting flat-state setup can re-fire on consecutive qualifying bars (each is technically a new signal). Alerts fire even when enable_orders=false (chart-tool mode), by design.
- State: Extensive var state, no varip. Regime: confirmed_core (570) commits only on confirmed bars with confirm_bars-run agreement loop (601-605); bars_in_regime (608), active_bars_in_regime (651). External-override trust latch: 20-bar in-set run to latch, >5 bad bars to unlatch (627-635). Markov: mk_counts 3x3 + mk_rows arrays (674-675) accumulate transitions from the first loaded bar with optional non-overlap stride sampling (679-697); dwell arrays capped at 120 completed spells per state (654-663), percentile inert until 5 completed spells (511-519). One-shot retest machines per side: short_bear_retest_seen/taken (1049-1057, consumed at 1612-1613), long_retest_seen/taken (1299-1307, consumed at 1579-1580) — reset on regime flip, latch after one trade. Trade plan state (plan_q*, leg_stop, run_stop, t1_done/t2_done, legs_placed, 958-1000) fully reset on flat. Blended virtual-equity ledger (915-935) attributes per-bar equity deltas by prior-bar side, with v3.6 fix booking entry-bar costs to the newly opened side. Warm-up: Mansfield needs mans_len=200 bars of RS ratio; vol percentile 252 bars; Markov gates need mk_cur_n >= 20 row samples; regime needs lookback_window + confirm_bars. na propagation is disciplined — nearly every gate requires not na(...) and na regime blocks entries.
- Translation feasibility: High for the core decision path. Faithfully translatable with daily/intraday OHLCV plus an aligned benchmark series: regime z-score engine with vol models, hysteresis and confirmation (521-621); Mansfield RS and new-high/low logic (743-758); HTF completed-bar filter (reproduce close[1] of resampled bars); divergence pivot logic (781-823); anchored VWAP with variance bands and stale guard (426-451, 826-851); all entry gates, both side systems, SHORT+/LONG+ Markov gates (fix data start to pin the transition counts); scoring; risk sizing; and a next-bar-open fill model for entries and confirmed-bar discretionary exits. Requires an explicit approximation policy: intra-bar stop/limit fills at chart TF need a tie-break rule (e.g., stop-first conservative) to replace bar magnifier; equity feedback (sizing/halts) is translatable but makes results path-dependent on the fill model; tick slippage becomes a per-fill cost assumption. Not translatable: external input.source override (must be dropped or fed a real series), all display layers. Expect small fill-level divergence from TradingView on bars where a target and stop are both touched; signal-level (entry/exit trigger) parity should be exact.
- Repaint risks:
  - No repainting in decision logic: entries, regime commits, retest state machines, dwell/Markov updates are all gated on barstate.isconfirmed (lines 604, 657, 664, 684, 845, 1051, 1301).
  - Intrabar, unconfirmed values of triggers/HUD/data-window exports (1736-1820, 1830+) flicker before close, but no order or alert consumes them: can_long/can_short embed confirmed (1467, 1471) and alert() uses freq_once_per_bar_close (2403, 2413, 2420, 2423).
  - Divergence pivots (788-823) confirm piv_r bars after the pivot — lag, not repaint; signals never revise once fired.
  - Same-TF benchmark security with lookahead_off (743) uses the benchmark's in-progress bar intrabar; final at close, so confirmed-bar entries are unaffected. Benchmark session mismatches with gaps_off carry the last value forward.
  - External regime override (135, 138, 625-640): if the linked external indicator repaints, this strategy inherits it; the 20-bar trust latch validates only the value domain {-1,0,1}, not repaint behavior.
  - Tables/HUD redraw on barstate.islast only — display, no signal revision. No label/box trail rewriting.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - Lines 772-773: security(tickerid, htf_req_tf, close[1], lookahead_on) — the standard NON-repainting prior-completed-HTF-bar idiom; offset [1] with lookahead_on is safe, NOT contaminating.
  - Lines 774-775: security(..., ta.ema(close, htf_ema_len)[1], lookahead_on) — same safe idiom applied to the HTF EMA; safe.
  - Line 771 fallback: when htf invalid, htf_req_tf = timeframe.period, so lookahead_on requests the chart TF with a [1] offset — still offset-protected, and the filter is force-disabled anyway via 'not htf_valid' in lines 776-777.
  - Lines 743-744: benchmark security uses lookahead_off with no offset — correct for same-timeframe requests; no future data.
  - No calc_on_order_fills, no process_orders_on_close (lines 34-35): orders placed at confirmed close fill at next bar open — no same-bar-close fill lookahead.
  - Markov/dwell statistics (674-717) are strictly causal: at bar t they only count transitions up to bar t; in-sample-fit concern for research, but not lookahead.
- Session/timezone:
  - Lines 1175-1176 and 1426-1427: optional session filters via time(timeframe.period, session) with default '0935-1530' and NO explicit timezone — interpreted in the exchange timezone of the chart symbol; DST is handled by the exchange for US equities but the window silently shifts for symbols on fixed-UTC exchanges (crypto). Both default OFF.
  - Line 901: day_time = time('D') for the daily-loss halt day boundary — exchange-timezone day; for 24/7 symbols the 'day' boundary is UTC-ish per exchange convention and DST-insensitive, but differs from a wall-clock trading day.
  - Lines 71-73: date-window and OOS timestamps declared explicitly in +0000 — unambiguous.
  - Gap-shock detector (590-594) and gap-up filters (1123-1125, 1385-1388) compare open vs prior close — behavior differs between 24/7 and session-gapped symbols by construction (intended).
  - Benchmark (SPY) vs a symbol with different sessions: gaps_off on line 744 forward-fills bench_close over non-overlapping bars, slightly staling Mansfield RS on holidays/half-days.
- TradingView-only:
  - use_bar_magnifier=true (line 36): intrabar fill sequencing for same-bar T1-limit vs stop conflicts uses lower-TF data unavailable to a chart-TF OHLCV backtester.
  - Broker emulator semantics: strategy.exit OCO leg behavior, tick-based slippage (line 31), and strategy.equity mark-to-market (used in sizing/halts, 895-935, 1554) must be re-implemented.
  - input.source external regime/strength links (lines 135, 138) — cross-indicator plot wiring has no off-platform equivalent.
  - request.security benchmark and HTF fetches (743-744, 772-775) — trivially replaced by aligned external data but platform-specific merge semantics (gaps_off forward-fill) must be replicated.
  - All table/HUD/validation dashboards and bgcolor/plotshape visuals (1734-2440) — display-only, no decision impact.
  - syminfo.pointvalue / syminfo.mintick dependencies (414-415, 1177, 1428) require instrument metadata.
  - alert()/alertcondition infrastructure (2392-2423).
- Known defects:
  - Lines 1651-1655: t1_done/t2_done are inferred from the leg entry ID no longer being open (f_entry_open), regardless of WHY it closed. A leg-1 exit at the shared leg stop (possible under bar-magnifier same-bar sequencing) would set t1_done and move remaining stops to breakeven as if T1 had been hit. Mostly moot because all non-runner legs share leg_stop, but it is exit-reason-blind bookkeeping.
  - Lines 929-934 (v3.6 attribution fix): if a position closes and the opposite side opens with fills on the same bar, the entire bar's equity delta (including the new side's entry commission) is attributed to the prior side; the fix only covers the flat-prior-bar case. Rare (entries require flat at signal), low impact.
  - Line 1557 vs line 1600 asymmetry: the long entry guard checks _stop0 > 0 but not _dist > 0, while the short guard checks _dist > 0. A zero/negative long stop distance is structurally near-impossible (long_struct_dist requires close > stop, line 1349) but the guard is inconsistent.
  - Lines 674-717 + 1134-1147: Markov gate probabilities are computed over ALL loaded chart history — entry behavior depends on TradingView's loaded-bar depth (max_bars_back 5000, line 37), harming run-to-run reproducibility across chart loads. Not a lookahead bug; a determinism defect.
  - Line 607: int_strength is named as int but is a float expression (na branch is float(na)) — cosmetic/misleading naming only.
  - Lines 2395-2397 vs 2398-2420: parallel alertcondition and alert() paths let a user double-subscribe the same signal (duplicate notification risk, documented as such, not a compile bug).
  - Line 404: profit-factor sentinel 999.0 for zero-gross-loss (rendered as infinity at line 406) leaks the literal 999 into any comparison against min_oos_pf_warn — benign in current use (only >= comparisons) but a magic-number hazard for translation.
  - Lines 900-912: daily-loss halt arms on any chart <= 1D including daily charts, where 'daily loss' degenerates to a per-bar loss check; semantics are questionable at exactly 1D (default OFF).
### 01 · Markov Regime BULL+ v1.1

- File: `research/pine/01_markov_regime_bull_plus.pine` · SHA-256 `efedcd5efe17b755…` · 1517 lines · Pine v6 · 142 inputs
- Classification: **strategy** · Family: **regime_detection** · Direction: long · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`PASS_WITH_CONSTRAINTS`**
- Explanation: The decision path is genuinely non-repainting and lookahead-safe as written: the sole security call is same-TF with lookahead_off (lines 712-713); the regime state machine mutates only under barstate.isconfirmed (lines 574-575, 581-587, 617-634); entries require barstate.isconfirmed (line 1083) with process_orders_on_close=false (line 55), giving next-bar-open fills; no varip; alerts fire once_per_bar_close on confirmed conditions (lines 1479-1496). It fails unconditional PASS on four constraints: (1) use_bar_magnifier=true (line 56) — intrabar stop-vs-limit resolution for the T1 limit vs leg_stop pairs (lines 1196-1198) depends on TradingView's lower-TF magnifier data and cannot be reproduced from chart OHLCV alone; (2) the Markov gate probabilities are expanding-window counts from the first loaded chart bar (lines 639-664, 844-847), so trade lists depend on how much history TradingView loads (plan/subscription dependent) — deterministic only if the data start is pinned; (3) sizing computes qty and stop from the signal bar's close (lines 1129-1131, 1106-1115) while the fill occurs at the next open, so realized per-trade risk deviates from risk_pct on gaps and the absolute stop _stop0 = signal close − dist is not repositioned to the fill; (4) the intraday session gate (lines 230, 1013) assumes US-equity exchange-timezone RTH. None of these repaint or leak future data; they bound faithful reproduction.
- Timeframes: Chart-timeframe adaptive via presets (lines 483-496): Auto maps <=4H charts to a 4H preset (lookback 30, enter z 1.10, confirm 3), >=weekly to Weekly, else Daily. Day-trading DNA (session micro-gate 0935-1530 line 230, daily-loss halt lines 785-797, liquidity filter) targets intraday US equities, but v1.1 explicitly made the session gate inert on daily/weekly (line 1013: `not timeframe.isintraday` auto-passes). Daily-loss halt is applicable on any TF <=1D (line 785) but degenerates to a no-op on Daily charts since every bar starts a new day and resets day_start_equity (lines 787-792). Volatility percentile needs 252 bars; Mansfield RS needs 200; Markov gate needs mkv_min_row_n=20 Bull-row transition samples, so effective warm-up is long and history-depth dependent.
- Symbols: US equities/ETFs implied: benchmark default AMEX:SPY (line 175), dollar-volume liquidity floor $1M (line 289), session string 0935-1530 in the chart symbol's exchange timezone. Handles no-volume symbols via equal-weight AVWAP option (line 191) and volume-dead blocking (lines 740-741). Uses syminfo.pointvalue for futures-style sizing (lines 357-358) and syminfo.mintick guard (line 1014). On 24h/non-US symbols the intraday session window (exchange tz) is semantically wrong hours; the strategy still runs but the gate blocks most of the day.
- External data: Exactly one request.security call, lines 712-713: request.security(bench_sym, timeframe.period, close, gaps=barmerge.gaps_off, lookahead=barmerge.lookahead_off) — SAME timeframe as the chart, different symbol (benchmark, default AMEX:SPY). No higher/lower-TF requests, no lookahead_on anywhere in the file. Feeds Mansfield relative strength (lines 714-723) and optional benchmark EMA risk filter (lines 726-729). Same-TF + lookahead_off is non-contaminating historically; gaps_off carries the last benchmark value across calendar mismatches (holidays), a deterministic but alignment-sensitive behavior for translation.
- Entries: Long-only bull-continuation. Prerequisites (all on confirmed bars): confirmed Bull regime from a vol-normalized log-return z-score engine with hysteresis, confirm-bar unanimity, EMA(100) direction filter, ADX/ER quality, and gap-shock neutralization (lines 483-607); Markov Bull-row gate — stay-probability (Raw/Wilson-LB/Laplace estimator) >= 60% with row n >= 20, optional age/maturity/dwell-percentile gates default OFF in v1.1 (lines 843-860); relative-strength requirement vs SPY (Mansfield leader/above-zero/new-high modes, lines 865-873); environment gates (equity-DD halt, daily-loss halt, cooldown, ATR%, $1M dollar-volume, AVWAP-alive, optional benchmark EMA, lines 838). Four setup triggers (lines 903-935): Leader Pullback (defended shelf + above AVWAP prior bar + defense candle), Bull-Flip Retest (one-shot after flip, AVWAP touch then reclaim), AVWAP Reclaim, VCP/Base Breakout (tight base + close above prior pivot). Blockers: no-chase (AVWAP stretch, RSI>=72, consecutive up-bars, stop too wide), resistance proximity, gap-up exhaustion (lines 960-985). Micro-gates: session, trigger-candle range <=1.4 ATR, close location >=0.55, volume >=1.05x MA (lines 1013-1019). Weighted continuation score must reach 72 (85 = A-grade, lines 1021-1052). Entry: three market legs L1/L2/L3 (~1/3 each) at next bar open, IDs encoding setup suffix (lines 1128-1157). Flat-only entry (line 1086); pyramiding=3 exists solely for the simultaneous legs.
- Exits: Initial protective stop _stop0 = signal close − dist, dist = structural (min of pullback/base shelf vs AVWAP − 0.25 ATR buffer, lines 937-944) or ATR(14)x2 fallback, placed per leg at entry (PROT_1..3, lines 1151-1157). On the first in-position bar close, PROT orders are cancelled and replaced (lines 1187-1200): leg1 exits at T1 = avg + 1R limit, leg2 at T2 = avg + 2R limit, both with leg_stop = avg − R; leg3 is a runner with stop-only run_stop, ratcheted (monotone max) by breakeven-after-T1 (avg + be_offset_r*R, lines 1176-1178) and a Chandelier trail (22-bar high − 3 ATR) activated after +1R (lines 1183-1186). Discretionary closes on confirmed bars: regime flips Bear (or Neutral if enabled) (lines 1202-1206), AVWAP failure via reclaim_dn (1208-1211), optional time stop (1213-1219). T1/T2 fill detection is inferred by leg absence via f_entry_open (lines 1172-1175), which lags one bar (see defects). Same-bar entry+stop-out on the fill bar is possible and resolved by bar magnifier.
- Sizing: Custom risk-based sizing (f_plan_qty, lines 1106-1115): qty = equity * (0.75% * risk_scale * conviction_mult) / (stop_dist * pointvalue), capped by notional cap_pct (100% equity), floored to qty_step with min_qty check. risk_scale = chop factor (1.0/0.75/0.45 by ADX+ER agreement) x mature-extension factor 0.70 (lines 705-707). Conviction multiplier 1.20x for A-grade vs 1.00x standard (lines 1054-1057). Position split into 3 legs of ~1/3 with min_qty-aware fallback merging (f_reset_plan, lines 1117-1126). Declared frictions: commission 0.03% + 2 ticks slippage (lines 49-51). Qty and stop computed from signal-bar close/equity, filled next open — gap risk deviation noted.
- Alerts: Disciplined. Two alertcondition() hooks (lines 1479-1480): entry-confirmed (can_enter, which embeds barstate.isconfirmed) and bull-flip. Dynamic alert() calls: entry alert in human-readable or JSON webhook form (input-selected, lines 1482-1493) and a flip alert gated by barstate.isconfirmed (1495-1496), all at alert.freq_once_per_bar_close — no intrabar duplicate-fire risk. JSON payload (line 1485) does not escape quotes in syminfo.ticker/variant_tag (low practical risk). Alerts and backtest use the same can_enter condition, so alert/backtest parity holds; note the alerted stop (line 1483) is signal-close-anchored while the real fill is next open.
- State: Heavy persistent state, all var (no varip): (1) Regime engine: confirmed_core (line 533) with hysteresis + confirm_bars unanimity loop (lines 571-572) committed only on isconfirmed (574); bars_in_regime (580) and active_bars_in_regime (609) confirmed-bar counters. (2) Markov module: mk_counts[9]/mk_rows[3] expanding-window transition counts (lines 639-664), stride 1 or lookback (line 645) — EXPANDING from first loaded bar, never windowed, so gate probabilities and therefore trades depend on total chart history depth; warm-up until mkv_min_row_n=20 Bull-row samples. (3) Dwell arrays per state capped at 120 via push/shift (lines 614-627), pushed on the confirmed flip bar using the PRIOR regime before the age counter resets — ordering verified correct (push block at 617 precedes reset block at 628). (4) One-shot retest machine long_retest_seen/long_retest_taken (lines 882-890) reset on flip_to_bull, latched taken at entry (line 1147). (5) Trade-plan block plan_q*/leg_stop/run_stop/t1_done/t2_done/legs_placed (lines 799-830) reset on position flat. (6) Equity peak (rolling vs latch, lines 778-783), daily halt latch (789-797), cooldown via last_exit_bar (799, 832-835). na propagation is defensively guarded throughout (explicit not-na checks on nearly every derived series).
- Translation feasibility: High for the decision layer, medium for the fill layer. Faithfully translatable to bar-close Python on OHLCV: regime z-score engine incl. StDev/EWMA/ATR% vol models and percentile adjustment (lines 498-518), hysteresis+confirmation state machine, ADX/ER/gap filters, expanding Markov transition counts with all three estimators, dwell arrays/percentile, Mansfield RS (needs an aligned benchmark close series replicating gaps_off carry-forward), regime-anchored VWAP with variance bands, all four setup triggers, blockers, micro-gates, scoring, sizing math, and the halt/cooldown machinery — all pure functions of confirmed bars plus deterministic state. Must pin the data start date to freeze the expanding Markov counts. Fill simulation caveats: (a) entries are next-bar-open markets — exact; (b) initial stop on the fill bar and the T1-limit-vs-stop race on subsequent bars are intrabar-path ambiguous — bar magnifier resolves these on TV; a Python port needs intraday data or a declared convention (conservative stop-first); (c) T1/T2 breakeven ratchet must replicate the one-bar detection lag (lines 1171-1178) to match TV, not the 'correct' same-bar version; (d) commission 0.03% + 2-tick slippage trivially replicable. Session gate needs exchange-tz-aware timestamps. Overall: the strategy's alpha logic is fully portable; only fill microstructure and history-window pinning need explicit conventions.
- Repaint risks:
  - None in the decision path: can_enter requires barstate.isconfirmed (line 1083) and calc_on_every_tick=false (line 53), so realtime signals only exist at bar close and never retract.
  - confirmed_core / bars_in_regime / dwell arrays / Markov counters mutate only inside barstate.isconfirmed blocks (lines 574, 581, 617, 628, 647-664) — historical and realtime paths agree.
  - bench_close (line 712) on the live realtime bar reflects the benchmark's unconfirmed close, so HUD/plots can wiggle intrabar, but no entry/exit decision reads it before confirmation — cosmetic only.
  - HUD, validation board, and halt banner redraw on barstate.islast (lines 1287, 1343, 1508) — display-layer only, no signal revision.
  - plotshape of can_enter (line 1246) can only appear at bar close and never disappears retroactively.
  - No varip anywhere; no label/box mutation of past bars.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - Line 712-713: request.security(..., lookahead=barmerge.lookahead_off) on the SAME timeframe — the safe form; no offset needed because no TF mismatch exists. Not contaminating.
  - No lookahead_on appears anywhere in the file; the risky idioms (higher-TF current value, lookahead_on without [1]) are absent.

Correct US-equity RTH for NYSE/NASDAQ symbols and DST-safe there (exchange tz tracks DST), but semantically wrong hours on non-US or 24h symbols (crypto: 09:35-15:30 UTC window is arbitrary).

- Known defects:

Known lag, must be replicated for parity, but it is a real divergence from the stated 'breakeven after T1' semantics.
  - Line 1147: long_retest_taken is latched only when setup_suffix == 'R'. If the Leader Pullback ('P', higher priority in the suffix ternary at 994-999) fires on a bar that ALSO satisfies the retest trigger, the one-shot retest stays armed and can fire again after the trade closes — the 'one-shot retest machine' contract (header line 34) is leaky in this overlap case.
  - Lines 1129-1131 vs fill: _stop0 = signal-bar close − dist is an absolute price; entry fills next open. On a gap-up open, actual risk per share exceeds plan_rdist and the stop sits farther than intended (risk_pct understated); on gap-down the stop can even be above the open. Standard TV pattern but a genuine risk-model discrepancy — flagged for translation.
  - Lines 785-792: daily_halt_applicable is true on Daily charts (chart_tf_sec <= 86400) but new_day is true every bar there, so day_start_equity resets each bar and the daily-loss halt can never trigger on Daily — silently inert despite the input being ON by default.   - Line 1013: on a chart whose exchange never intersects '0935-1530' local time (24h symbols in odd timezones are fine, but e.g. a session string outside exchange hours), mg_session_ok is permanently false and the strategy silently produces zero trades — same failure family as the v1.0 daily bug this version fixed, just relocated to symbol/session mismatch. No warning is surfaced.
  - Line 1092: the 'wouldbe' blocker telemetry counts blk1/blk2/blk3 among bars passing permission+RS+setup, but does not condition on mkgate/micro-gates/score, so counters overstate the marginal impact of the three blockers relative to actual entry opportunity.

  - Lines 962: long_too_many_up_bars compares long_up_count >= long_up_bars_len where the loop (956-958) counts up-closes over exactly long_up_bars_len comparisons — condition requires ALL bars up; consistent, but the input name 'Consecutive up-bar window' implies a threshold separate from the window; parameter semantics are conflated (window == threshold).

### 02 · Markov Regime BEAR+ v1.1

- File: `research/pine/02_markov_regime_bear_plus.pine` · SHA-256 `6736df27aa13e827…` · 1507 lines · Pine v6 · 141 inputs

- Explanation: The script's own decision logic is genuinely non-repainting and lookahead-safe: regime state commits only on barstate.isconfirmed (577-578), regime age (584-590), dwell push (620-630), the one-shot retest machine (896-902), can_enter (1072-1076), discretionary closes (1193, 1198, 1206) and alerts (1472-1486, freq_once_per_bar_close) are all confirmed-bar; the single security call (715-716) is same-TF lookahead_off; strategy() uses calc_on_every_tick=false / process_orders_on_close=false (56-58) so fills are next-bar-open and historical/realtime behavior matches. Constraints that prevent a clean PASS: (1) path dependence on loaded history depth — the Markov transition matrix (642-667) and dwell arrays (617-630) accumulate from the first loaded bar, so the same settings on a different data-window depth produce different gate decisions (mkgate_row_n >= 20 gate at 852); this is not lookahead but it is environment-nondeterminism. (2) use_bar_magnifier=true (line 59) plus simultaneous limit+stop legs (X1_T1/X2_T2 at 1186-1188) makes same-bar exit adjudication depend on TradingView's intrabar engine — irreproducible at bar level. (3) The daily-loss halt is effectively inert on Daily charts: daily_halt_applicable is true for chart_tf_sec <= 24h (788) but new_day is true on every daily bar (790), so day_start_equity resets each bar (793-795) and daily_dd_pct (796-798) is always ~0 — the header's "applicable" claim overstates it. (4) Sizing/halts feed off strategy.equity (782-800, 1101-1102), so signals are coupled to the simulated fill path. None of these are repainting defects; they are translation and reproducibility constraints.
- Timeframes: Timeframe-adaptive via preset auto-mapping (lines 486-499): charts <=4H get the 4H preset (lookback 30, enter_z 1.10, confirm 3), >=1W get Weekly, else Daily. Intraday-first design: session micro-gate default "0935-1530" (line 233) applies only when timeframe.isintraday (line 1004 — the v1.1 fix; v1.0 produced zero trades on Daily). Daily-loss halt is declared applicable for chart_tf_sec <= 24h (line 788). Works on daily but several protections degrade there (see session_timezone_risks).
- Symbols: US equity assumed: benchmark default AMEX:SPY (line 178), session default 0935-1530 matches US cash session in exchange tz. Volume-bearing symbol expected for AVWAP; explicit fallback for no-volume symbols (vwap_weighting "Equal", line 194) and a block-if-volume-AVWAP-dead guard (line 195, 743-744). Dollar-volume liquidity floor $1M default (line 292). syminfo.pointvalue used in sizing if enabled (lines 360-361), so futures are nominally supported.
- External data: Exactly one request.security call, line 715-716: bench_close = request.security(bench_sym, timeframe.period, close, gaps=barmerge.gaps_off, lookahead=barmerge.lookahead_off). Same-timeframe (chart TF), lookahead_off, no offset needed because the target is the chart's own timeframe — this is not the HTF-unconfirmed-value pattern and is not contaminating. It feeds the Mansfield RS module (717-726) and optional benchmark EMA filter (729-732). No higher/lower TF requests, no VIX, no lookahead_on anywhere in the file.
- Entries: Short-only continuation entries, confirmed bar, next-bar-open market fills. Stack required (1072-1076): confirmed Bear regime (z-score of lookback log-return vs realized vol, EMA-direction filter, hysteresis, N-bar confirmation, gap-shock neutralization); entry gates (quality ADX/ER, strength >=55, min bars in regime, no shock bar, 703-705); environment (no equity-DD/daily-loss halt, cooldown, ATR% and $-volume floors, AVWAP alive, optional benchmark below EMA, 840-841); Markov Bear-row gate (856-863): stay-probability >=60% under selectable Raw/Wilson-LB/Laplace estimator, row n>=20, regime age <=12 bars, maturity (age / geometric mean dwell) <=1.0, optional dwell-percentile youth gate; one of three setups — Failed AVWAP Reclaim (890-891), Bear-Flag Breakdown below prior-bar shelf floor (914-920), one-shot Bear-Flip First Retest (904-912) — each also requiring the selected relative-weakness mode and not rs_leader (872-877); anti-chase blocks (no-chase RSI/stretch/down-bar-run/stop-width, support-proximity room, squeeze risk: green body / gap-up / high ATR% / RS recovery, 959-990); intraday micro-gates (session window, trigger-bar range <=1.4 ATR, lower-half close, volume expansion, 1004-1010); additive continuation score >=72 (1012-1027). Must be flat (position_size == 0) — pyramiding=3 exists only to admit the three simultaneous legs of one signal.
- Exits: Three-leg exit plan. On entry, protective stops PROT_1..3 at signal close + stop distance (structural: max(shelf high, AVWAP) + 0.25 ATR buffer, else 2x ATR fallback; 1119-1147). Once in position, PROT_* are cancelled and replaced (1177-1190): leg 1 limit at T1 = 1R, leg 2 limit at T2 = 2R (t2_eff = max(t2,t1), line 1093), leg 3 runner on a ratcheting stop; breakeven move on leg-stop after T1 fills (detected by scanning open trades, 1162-1165), with configurable BE offset R (1166-1168); Chandelier trail (lowest(low,22) + 3 ATR) activates after 1R profit and only tightens (1173-1176). If quantity could not split into 3 legs, a single X1_ALL exit carries T2 limit + runner stop (1182-1183). Discretionary confirmed-bar closes: regime flips to Bull (or Neutral if enabled) (1192-1196), AVWAP reclaim (1198-1201), optional max-bars time stop (1203-1209). Same-bar entry/stop-out on the fill bar is possible and adjudicated by the bar magnifier.
- Sizing: Custom risk sizing (f_plan_qty, 1096-1105): qty = equity * (risk_pct 0.75% x chop/extension risk_scale (708-710) x conviction multiplier 1.20 A-grade / 1.00 standard) / (stop distance x point value), capped by max position value % of equity (default 100%), floored/stepped by min_qty/qty_step, split into thirds with remainder handling (f_reset_plan, 1107-1116). Sizing uses strategy.equity, so it is backtest-path-dependent. Commission 0.03% + 2 ticks slippage declared (52-54). Initial capital 100k.
- Alerts: Two alertcondition hooks (1469-1470: setup confirmed, bear flip) plus runtime alert() calls: entry alert in human-readable or JSON webhook form (1472-1483) and a regime-flip alert (1485-1486), all at alert.freq_once_per_bar_close and inside isconfirmed-gated conditions — confirmed-bar discipline is correct and per-bar duplicate risk is low. Residual duplicate risk: a user who enables BOTH the alertcondition and the alert() path for the same event gets two notifications per signal; also the entry alert's stop is recomputed at 1473 identically to the order's stop, so no divergence there. JSON payload (1475) builds valid JSON assuming setup_name/ticker contain no quotes.
- State: Heavy but disciplined persistent state, all confirmed-bar: (1) confirmed_core regime latch with hysteresis + confirm-bars loop (536-578); (2) bars_in_regime / active_bars_in_regime age counters (583-590, 612-637); (3) Markov machinery — mk_counts 3x3 flattened array(9), mk_rows(3), last-sample regime/bar, optional non-overlap stride sampling (642-667); (4) dwell-time arrays capped at 120 spells per state, pushed on flip bar before age reset with if/else array selection (617-630, replacing a flagged ternary compile risk); (5) one-shot Bear-Flip Retest machine short_bear_retest_seen/taken, reset on flip_to_bear, consumed only when suffix == "R" fires (894-902, 1137-1138) — note precedence: if Failed-Reclaim ("F") and Retest fire on the same bar, suffix is "F" and the retest is NOT consumed (intentional per header line 22); (6) trade-plan block plan_q/q1/q2/q3, leg_stop, run_stop, legs_placed, t1_done/t2_done, fully reset on flat (802-833); (7) equity_peak latch vs rolling window (781-786); (8) AVWAP accumulators inside f_avwap via function-scoped var (375-400), anchored on regime flips. Warm-up is substantial: mkgate needs >=20 Bear-row transitions, Mansfield needs 200 bars of benchmark ratio, vol percentile 252 bars — and the Markov/dwell state never converges to a history-independent value, so signal onset depends on chart history depth (max_bars_back 5000, line 60). na propagation is carefully guarded throughout (explicit not-na checks before nearly every comparison).
- Translation feasibility: High for the decision layer, medium for the execution layer. Faithfully translatable from chart-TF OHLCV plus a same-TF SPY series: regime z-score engine incl. StDev/EWMA/ATR% vol models and percentrank threshold adjustment (501-521), hysteresis + confirm-bars state machine (536-578), Markov transition counting with Raw/Wilson/Laplace estimators (642-692, 428-453), dwell percentile, Mansfield RS (715-726), anchored VWAP with variance (375-400), all three setups, every filter/block, micro-gates, the continuation score, and the confirmed-bar discretionary exits — all are pure bar-close arithmetic. Requires care: fix a warm-up convention for the Markov/dwell state (results depend on where history starts); replicate ta.rma/ta.atr Wilder smoothing and ta.percentrank exactly; exchange calendar for the session gate and time(\"D\") day boundaries. Not faithfully translatable at bar level: same-bar ordering of T1 limit vs leg stop vs runner stop on the fill bar (bar-magnifier-dependent) — a Python port must adopt an explicit conservative tie-break rule (e.g. stop-first) and will diverge from TradingView's equity curve; equity-coupled sizing/halts translate mechanically but inherit any fill divergence. Recommend porting signals + next-open fills with stop-first pessimistic adjudication and treating TV backtest numbers as non-authoritative.

### 03 · KLQuant Shared Library v1.1

- File: `research/pine/03_klquant_shared_library.pine` · SHA-256 `d10a0a4fbbaf50c3…` · 289 lines · Pine v6 · 0 inputs
- Classification: **library** · Family: **statistical_readout** · Direction: none · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR`**
- Explanation: This is a Pine v6 library() declaration (line 35) exporting pure math and stateful session/statistics utilities — it contains no strategy() calls, no entries/exits, no plots of signals, and no alertcondition/alert(). Having no trade rules is its design, not a defect. The library's own logic is clean on the forensic axes: zero request.security calls anywhere (no lookahead vector); the one barstate usage, barstate.isconfirmed gating the Markov transition update at line 233, is the correct confirmed-bar discipline (historical bars are always confirmed, realtime counts only mutate on bar close — historical and realtime paths agree); tod_baseline (lines 156-175) explicitly EXCLUDES today's row from the average (line 170 'if r != row'), so the RVOL/noise-band baselines are built only from completed prior days — no self-referential lookahead. The residual risks are caller-side contracts, not internal bugs: every var-stateful export (day_bar, tod_baseline, day_cum_volume, rvol_tod, noise_bands, markov_stay, gap_class, squeeze_bars) must be called unconditionally every bar (warned at lines 128-130), and intrabar values of rvol_tod/noise_bands/squeeze_on naturally fluctuate until bar close because they read live close/volume — the caller must apply confirmed-bar discipline to signals derived from them. Not PASS because PASS is reserved for complete decision logic, which this file intentionally lacks; not REQUIRES_REWRITE because no internal defect demands one.
- Timeframes: Fixed intraday timeframe explicitly assumed for the session/time-of-day machinery (comment lines 125-130): day_bar()/tod_baseline() index bars by ordinal position within the calendar day, so bar-of-day only aligns across days if the timeframe and session template never change. rth_fraction() (lines 146-150) hardcodes a 6.5h 09:30-16:00 US equity RTH clock. squeeze_on/markov_stay/wilson helpers are timeframe-agnostic. Nothing multi-timeframe: zero request.security calls in the file.
- Symbols: US equity RTH data feed assumed for session helpers (line 127 'RTH data feeds align'; lines 146-150 hardcoded 09:30-16:00). day-boundary detection via ta.change(time("D")) (line 134) works on any symbol but bar-of-day alignment breaks on 24h/crypto/futures with irregular sessions or extended-hours charts. Volume-dependent exports (day_cum_volume line 178, rvol_tod line 187) require a symbol with real volume. Math exports (Wilson, Laplace, Markov, squeeze) are symbol-agnostic.
- External data: None. No request.security(), no other tickers, no higher/lower timeframe fetches. The only resolution reference is time("D") at line 134, which is the chart-local daily bar open time used purely as a calendar-day-change detector — not a data request and not a lookahead vector.
- Entries: None. Library only — exports estimators (Wilson bounds lines 58-65, stay_wilson_lb/stay_laplace/stay_gate_prob lines 75-92), session/RVOL machinery (lines 133-190), noise bands (lines 203-215), Markov stay-probability (lines 229-247), gap classification (lines 256-268), and squeeze detection (lines 274-284). Importing scripts supply all entry logic.
- Exits: None. No exit, stop, or target logic; dwell_push/dwell_percentile (lines 103-122) provide regime-age percentile machinery that importers may use for time-based exits, but the library itself makes no exit decision.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing. pf_text/pf_num (lines 46-51) are profit-factor formatters for journaling displays, not sizing functions.
- Alerts: No alertcondition() or alert() calls. Alert behavior is entirely the importer's responsibility.
- State: Heavy per-call-site var state, all correctly scoped (Pine gives each export call site its own state instance): day_bar var int (line 138); tod_baseline var matrix<float> days x max_bars plus var row pointer with ring rotation and row-clear on new day (lines 157-163); day_cum_volume var float accumulator (line 179); noise_bands and gap_class each hold var d_open (lines 204, 257); markov_stay holds a flat 9-cell transition-count array, 3-cell row-total array, and prev sentinel -99 (lines 230-232) with the v1.1 na-resets-chain fix (lines 234-235) so regime gaps do not fabricate transitions; squeeze_bars var counter (line 282). Call discipline is the critical contract (lines 128-130): any stateful export invoked inside an `if` desynchronizes both its var state and the internal ta.* warm-up. Warm-up requirements: tod_baseline needs up to `days` completed days before dense baselines; dwell_percentile refuses to answer below 5 recorded spells (lines 116-117); rvol_tod/noise_bands return na until baseline exists; wilson/laplace return na on n<=0. na propagation is disciplined throughout — unknowns return na rather than fake zeros (gap_class v1.1 fix, lines 254, 261).
- Translation feasibility: High — this is the most translatable file in the suite. Every export is a pure function of OHLCV plus explicit state: Wilson/Laplace/gate-prob estimators (lines 58-92) are closed-form arithmetic; dwell ring + percentile (lines 103-122) is a bounded deque; the Markov 3x3 transition counter (lines 229-247) is a dict update; gap_class (lines 256-268) needs only prior daily close and ATR; noise_bands (lines 203-215) and tod_baseline/rvol_tod (lines 156-190) translate to a pandas groupby on (date, bar-of-day) with a shift to exclude the current day — arguably MORE robust in Python since half-day misalignment can be fixed by keying on clock time instead of bar ordinal. squeeze_on needs a faithful Wilder-RMA ATR and population-vs-sample stdev match (Pine ta.stdev is population). Only intrabar realtime behavior (live rvol_tod ticks) is untranslatable to bar-close OHLCV, and the library's own doctrine already treats confirmed-bar values as canonical.
- Repaint risks:
  - No varip, no security, no label/box drawing anywhere in the file — the classic repaint vectors are absent.
  - Intrabar mutability, caller-side: tod_baseline (line 165) overwrites today's cell with the live `value` on every tick and rvol_tod (lines 187-190) reads live cumulative volume, so their realtime values move until bar close. Historical/realtime parity is preserved at bar close because the baseline excludes today (line 170); only unconfirmed intrabar reads differ.
  - squeeze_on (lines 274-278) uses live close via ta.sma/ta.stdev/ta.atr — its boolean can flip intrabar; squeeze_bars (lines 281-284) increments from it, so a caller reading it before bar close sees revisable state.
  - markov_stay is the one export with internal confirmed-bar gating (line 233) and is therefore non-repainting by construction; the returned [p, rn, sc] are stable within a bar.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - No request.security calls exist, so neither the contaminating lookahead_on-without-offset pattern nor the safe expr[1]+lookahead_on idiom appears — nothing to distinguish.
  - tod_baseline line 170: the r != row guard excludes today's partially-filled row from the average, so the time-of-day baseline uses only prior completed days — explicitly lookahead-safe.
  - noise_bands sigma (lines 206-207) feeds today's |close/open-1| into the matrix but the same exclusion means today's move never contaminates today's sigma — matches the Zarattini/Barbon paper's prior-N-days formulation.
  - day_open anchoring (noise_bands line 205, gap_class line 258) captures `open` on the first bar of the day — known at session start, no future information.
- Session/timezone:
  - Lines 146-150 (rth_fraction): hour/minute resolve in the exchange timezone, so 09:30-16:00 is correct for US equities including DST shifts, but the function silently returns clamped garbage (0 or a wrong fraction) on any non-US-equity venue; the comment says 'supply your own' but the code has no venue guard.
  - Line 134 (new_day): ta.change(time("D")) uses calendar-day change, not session strings — on futures/crypto the 'day' boundary follows the symbol's daily-bar convention (e.g. CME 17:00 CT rollover), which may not match the caller's mental model of a session.
  - Lines 125-130 documented assumption: bar-of-day indexing (day_bar/tod_baseline) breaks if the chart includes extended hours, half-days (early closes), or data-feed gaps — a Thanksgiving half-day shifts every subsequent bar-of-day slot for that day, polluting the tod_baseline matrix row for `days` days.
  - ta.change(time("D")) is na on the very first bar of the dataset, so new_day() is false there; day-anchored state (d_open at lines 205 and 258) stays na for the entire first calendar day — a warm-up gap, not an error.
- TradingView-only:
  - library()/export mechanism itself (line 35) — the publish-and-import distribution model (line 13) is TradingView-only, though the math ports trivially.
  - Per-call-site var/matrix state instancing semantics (e.g. tod_baseline line 157) — Pine's implicit one-state-per-call-site behavior must be made explicit as class instances in Python.
  - barstate.isconfirmed (line 233) — meaningless in bar-close-only Python; simply always true.
  - Built-in ta.change/ta.sma/ta.stdev/ta.atr and time("D") calendar semantics — replicable but ta.atr is RMA-smoothed true range and time("D") day-boundary conventions per asset class must be reproduced exactly.
- Known defects:
  - Lines 58-65 (wilson_lo/wilson_hi): no clamp on p outside [0,1]; a caller passing a corrupted proportion >1 makes p*(1-p) negative and math.sqrt returns na silently rather than erroring — acceptable na propagation but undocumented.
  - Lines 274-278 (squeeze_on): uses ta.sma/ta.stdev/ta.atr yet carries no 'call every bar' warning, unlike the stateful exports (contrast lines 128-130); a caller invoking it conditionally silently corrupts the ta.* series history — the library's own call-discipline doc misses this export.
  - Line 134 (new_day): ta.change(time("D")) is na on bar 0, so the first bar of the dataset is never flagged as a new day; d_open in noise_bands (line 205) and gap_class (line 258) stays na through the entire first day — warm-up quirk, undocumented.
  - Lines 146-150 (rth_fraction): no venue guard — on non-US-equity symbols it returns a clamped but meaningless value instead of na; the limitation is only in a comment ('Other venues: supply your own').
  - Lines 156-163 (tod_baseline): if the chart's session template changes (e.g. user toggles extended hours) mid-history, stale rows keyed to the old bar-of-day alignment blend into baselines for up to `days` days with no invalidation mechanism.
  - Line 43 (fmt): dp > 4 silently falls through to 4 decimals (format string '#.####'); doc promises 0-4 but does not state the >4 clamp — cosmetic.
  - No compile-blocking defects found: no use-before-declare, ternary type mismatches, or unguarded divisions (all divisions guarded: lines 47, 51, 76, 83, 92, 175, 190, 206, 246, 262-264).

### 04 · SIP RVOL Screener v1.1

- File: `research/pine/04_sip_rvol_screener.pine` · SHA-256 `5132ccb04310994d…` · 338 lines · Pine v6 · 22 inputs
- Classification: **indicator** · Family: **volume_orderflow** · Direction: none · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR`**
- Explanation: This is a selection screener with no entry/exit/order logic — indicator() declared at line 39, no strategy.* calls anywhere; that is by design, not a defect. Its own decision logic (the boolean "In-Play" flag, f_inplay lines 178-182) is essentially non-repainting and lookahead-safe for its stated RTH use: daily context uses the canonical safe idiom expr[1] + lookahead_on (lines 144-145, 213-214); the one un-offset daily series fetched with lookahead_on is `open`, which is immutable once the RTH session has started, so it is not contaminating on an RTH chart — but on an extended-hours chart it forward-peeks the 9:30 RTH open during premarket bars (see lookahead_risks). Intraday fetches use lookahead_off on the chart timeframe (line 140), which updates intrabar in realtime by design (screener semantics) and is confirmed-final on history. The exact time-of-day matrix writes are confirm-gated (barstate.isconfirmed, line 199) and today's row is excluded from the baseline (r != tod_row, line 205), so the denominator never self-contaminates. Alerts are double-gated (isconfirmed + alert.freq_once_per_bar_close, lines 300-306, 321-323). Remaining issues are approximation/edge-case grade (half-days, premarket, bar-index vs clock-time alignment, rvol_days input not driving the basket SMA length) and are catalogued in defects/risks, not integrity killers.
- Timeframes: Designed for intraday RTH charts of US equities (author suggests 1-minute; tod_max_bars default 400 sized for 390 one-minute RTH bars, line 73-74). Proration functions f_rth_fraction (lines 113-115) and f_u_fraction (lines 120-127) hardcode a 9:30-16:00, 390-minute session. The exact time-of-day RVOL matrix aligns by bar-index-within-day (day_b, lines 189-210), so any timeframe works mechanically, but the semantics assume a fixed, complete regular session. On a daily or higher chart the proration and tod machinery degenerate (fraction clamps, day_b always 0).
- Symbols: US large-cap equities. The 10-symbol default basket is SPY/QQQ + megacaps (lines 52-61). hour/minute are exchange-timezone, so the 9:30-16:00 math only holds for US-listed symbols; a non-US chart symbol or basket member silently gets a wrong session-fraction. U-shape volume curve (line 121) is a stylized US large-cap profile, honestly labeled as an approximation. Dollar-volume floor default $20M/day assumes liquid equities.
- External data: 21 request.security calls: per basket symbol (10 symbols x 2) — f_intraday (line 139) fetches [session cum volume, ta.vwap, close] on the CHART timeframe with lookahead_off/gaps_off; f_daily (lines 143-145) fetches "D" [close[1], sma(vol,20)[1], atr(14)[1], EMA20-trend[1], open] with lookahead_on. Plus one "D" call for the chart symbol itself (lines 212-214, same tuple). No VIX or other index data. 21 calls is within TradingView's 40-call limit but is a heavy fanout.
- Entries: None — no orders. The nearest thing to a signal is the In-Play flag (f_inplay, lines 178-182): RVOL >= threshold (default 1.5x) AND 20d avg dollar volume >= floor (default $20M) AND optional |gap| >= N ATRs. Basket rows use prorated RVOL (cum vol / (20d avg x session fraction, U-shape or linear)); the chart symbol uses exact time-of-day RVOL (cum vol vs avg cum vol at the same bar-of-day over prior N days). This is candidate selection, explicitly framed as decision support (lines 37, 275).
- Exits: None. No exits, stops, or targets exist or are implied.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing of any kind.
- Alerts: Two alertcondition()s, both confirm-gated in the condition itself: chart symbol crossing into In-Play (line 300, edge-triggered via inplay_new = chart_inplay and not chart_inplay[1], line 299) and basket In-Play count increase (line 321). Parallel dynamic alert() calls (lines 301-306, 322-323) are gated by barstate.isconfirmed AND alert.freq_once_per_bar_close — no intrabar duplicate-fire risk. Optional JSON payload (input line 81, emitted line 303) with na-to-null handling. Residual chattiness: neither alert is one-shot per day — a symbol that oscillates around the RVOL threshold across bar closes re-fires on each re-cross, and basket_count_up re-fires whenever the count dips then recovers.
- State: Persistent state: var matrix<float> tod_m 60x500 (line 187) — ring buffer of per-day cumulative-volume curves; tod_row rotates modulo _days_eff on each new day (line 196) and the incoming row is cleared to na (lines 197-198). var day_b / chart_cum reset on new day (lines 189-192). Inside security, f_day_cum_vol (lines 130-133) carries a var accumulator per fetched symbol — legal and correct in v6. Warm-up: exact tod RVOL needs prior days in the matrix; HUD warm-gate at tod_n >= 5 (line 290) is honest labeling, and chart_rvol_tod is na until tod_n>0 (line 210). Basket RVOL needs ~20 daily bars for sma(volume,20) and 14 for ATR; na propagation is disciplined — every derived metric checks not na(...) and positive-denominator guards (lines 151-157, 215-216), and f_inplay treats na liquidity as false (line 180). scr_merged one-shot flag (lines 276-279) guards table.merge_cells. First-bar edge: day_b initialized -1; ta.change is na on bar 1 so f_new_day is false and day_b becomes 0 — works, but relies on na-comparison-is-false semantics.
- Translation feasibility: High — the entire decision core is faithfully translatable to Python given per-symbol intraday bars plus daily OHLCV: session cumulative volume, 20d SMA of daily volume, ATR(14), EMA(20) daily trend, gap vs prior close, linear and U-shape (14-knot piecewise-linear, line 121) proration, the exact time-of-day RVOL ring-buffer (keyed by clock time rather than bar index — an improvement over the Pine bar-index proxy), and the three-condition In-Play predicate. Session VWAP replicates from hlc3*vol cumsum. In backtest, only use each bar's completed values (equivalent to the confirmed-bar path); the intrabar-flicker realtime behavior is not replicable from bar data and should be modeled as evaluate-at-bar-close. Fix the premarket/half-day/extended-hours edge cases explicitly in the port. TradingView-only: all rendering, alerts, and the security-fanout mechanics.
- Repaint risks:
  - Realtime-vs-history divergence, by design: f_intraday (line 139) returns the current unconfirmed bar's cum volume/vwap/close in realtime, so basket RVOL, VWAP side, and In-Play flags fluctuate intrabar and settle at bar close; historical bars only ever show the settled value. Table/HUD render only on barstate.islast (lines 228, 286), so the display always reflects the live, possibly-unconfirmed bar.
  - chart_cum (line 192) and chart_rvol_tod (line 210) include the current unconfirmed bar's volume in the numerator intrabar; only the matrix baseline is confirm-gated. Intrabar In-Play state can therefore flicker on/off; the alert path is protected by the isconfirmed gate but the on-screen flag is not.
  - No varip, no label/box redrawing, no security of an unconfirmed higher-TF value. The tod matrix write at line 199-200 overwrites the same cell (tod_row, day_b) — but only once per bar index and only when confirmed, so stored history does not revise.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - Lines 144-145 and 212-214: request.security(..., "D", [close[1], sma[1], atr[1], trend[1], open], lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on). The [1]-offset series are the standard safe non-repainting idiom — NOT contaminating. The un-offset `open` is safe on RTH intraday charts because the daily open is fixed at the first bar of the day and lookahead_on then merely delivers today's already-printed open.
  - Extended-hours contamination: on a chart with premarket/afterhours enabled, premarket bars belong to the same daily bar, and lookahead_on delivers the daily (RTH) open — a value that has not printed yet at e.g. 08:00. Gap%, Gap/ATR, and any gap-gated In-Play flag are forward-looking during premarket in both backtest and replay. The script assumes RTH-only charts but never enforces it (no session filter).
  - No other lookahead vectors: intraday security is lookahead_off (line 140); matrix baseline excludes today (line 205).
- Session/timezone:
  - Lines 113-115, 122: session boundaries hardcoded as minutes-from-9:30 over 390 minutes using hour/minute (exchange timezone). Correct for US equities across DST since exchange tz is America/New_York, but silently wrong for any non-US symbol on the chart.
  - Half-days (1:00pm closes): both proration curves still assume a 390-min day, so end-of-half-day RVOL is understated (fraction ~0.53-0.59 at 13:00 while the day is complete). The exact tod matrix also misaligns: a 13:00 close writes only ~210 one-minute cells, and the next full day's afternoon bars compare against na cells from the short day (tod_n drops, handled gracefully but the baseline thins).
  - Premarket bars: _mins < 570 clamps the fraction to 0.02-0.04 (lines 115, 127), so premarket cum volume divided by 2-4% of a day's average volume produces enormous prorated RVOL — combined with the premarket gap lookahead above, the screener is unreliable before 09:30 on extended-hours charts.
  - f_new_day (line 111) keys off ta.change(time("D")) — robust to holidays/weekends; no session-string inputs anywhere.
  - Bar-index alignment (day_b, line 191) is a proxy for clock time: a data gap or missing bar early in a day shifts every subsequent bar's comparison column by one, degrading the 'exact' tod RVOL silently.
- TradingView-only:
  - request.security fanout: 21 calls across 11 symbols (lines 139-145, 160-169, 212-214) — the multi-symbol screener architecture itself
  - input.symbol basket configuration (lines 52-61)
  - table.* screener grid and HUD incl. table.merge_cells (lines 222-294)
  - alertcondition/alert() plumbing and webhook JSON (lines 300-323)
  - Data Window plot exports as an output channel (lines 328-333)
  - ta.vwap (session-anchored VWAP) fetched inside security (line 139) — needs intraday hlc3*volume cumsum to replicate
  - barstate.isconfirmed / barstate.islast realtime semantics
- Known defects:
  - Line 72 vs 144/213: the 'RVOL lookback (days)' input (rvol_days) does NOT drive the basket 20d average — ta.sma(volume, 20) is hardcoded (it cannot take a non-simple length inside this construction, but the UI implies it is configurable). rvol_days only affects the chart-symbol tod matrix depth (_days_eff, line 193). Input label is misleading.
  - Lines 144-145/212-214: un-offset daily `open` under lookahead_on forward-peeks the RTH open on premarket bars of extended-hours charts; no session guard exists (design assumes RTH charts, unenforced).
  - Lines 115/127: premarket/pre-9:30 bars clamp the session fraction to 0.02/0.04, producing wildly inflated prorated RVOL rather than na — a truthful na would be safer.
  - Line 191: day_b is bar-index-within-day, not clock time; any missing/extra bar (data gap, half-day) shifts tod-matrix column alignment for the rest of the day — the 'exact' RVOL silently degrades.
  - Lines 113-127: 390-minute session hardcoded; half-days understate late-session RVOL for both proration modes and thin the tod baseline.
  - Line 154: intraday dollar volume approximates with cum_vol x current price (not vwap x cum_vol) — mild bias, cosmetic column only.
  - Lines 241-248: insertion sort's na handling shifts na rows down but a na key (_kv na) never advances past non-na rows only because the while condition short-circuits — correct, but fragile; verified sound as written.
  - Line 320: nz(basket_inplay_count[1]) means the first confirmed bar with any in-play symbol always fires the count-up alert on script load/chart open — a spurious first-fire.
  - No compile-level defects found: security barmerge args are keyword-form (lines 140, 145, 214 — the v1.0 positional bug the header describes is fixed), merge_cells is one-shot guarded (lines 276-279), all divisions guarded.

### 05 · Session Structure & ORB v1.1

- File: `research/pine/05_session_structure_orb.pine` · SHA-256 `b6eb244e47aaca4e…` · 325 lines · Pine v6 · 20 inputs
- Classification: **indicator** · Family: **market_structure** · Direction: bidirectional · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`PASS_WITH_CONSTRAINTS`**
- Explanation: The script's own decision logic is clean: the single security call (lines 117-118) uses the safe [1]+lookahead_on prior-day idiom; break signals require or_done AND barstate.isconfirmed AND close beyond OR (lines 210-211), so nothing repaints; retest latches (224-227) and day-type export (239-241) are confirmed-bar gated; alerts use freq_once_per_bar_close (313-320). Constraints preventing an unqualified PASS: (1) same-bar break+retest — the break sets break_dir/long_fired at lines 214-221 and the retest block at 224-227 runs later in the same bar's execution, so the breakout bar itself can immediately register "RETESTED" if its extreme lies within tolerance of the OR level; (2) session/timezone assumptions (exchange-time session string, line 48; ETH-inclusive daily feed vs RTH session for pd levels, line 117); (3) OR window quantization on non-divisor timeframes (149-153) with only a tf>OR warning at 114/286; (4) nz(atr_val) at lines 209/223 silently zeroes the break buffer and retest tolerance during ATR warm-up. It is a signal indicator, not a strategy — no orders, exits, or sizing exist, which is by design, not a defect.
- Timeframes: Intraday chart with timeframe <= or_minutes (default 5). Line 113-114 computes tf_min and warns (HUD, line 286) if tf > OR window, but only warns — OR degrades to first-bar granularity. Non-divisor TFs (e.g. 2m chart, 5m OR) silently over-extend the OR window to the next bar boundary (lines 148-153). Daily security call (line 117) supplies prior-day context regardless of chart TF.
- Symbols: US equity RTH assumed: default session "0930-1600" in exchange time (line 48). Prior-day H/L/C come from the "D" feed (line 117), which on futures/crypto includes ETH — mismatched with an RTH session string. Requires volume for VWAP (lines 167-178; svwap is na on volumeless symbols, degrading day-type and VWAP exports, guarded via cum_v > 0 at line 178).
- External data: One request.security call (lines 117-118): syminfo.tickerid on "D" returning [high[1], low[1], close[1], ta.atr(14)[1]] with gaps_off and lookahead_on — the standard non-repainting completed-prior-day idiom. No other tickers, no VIX, no lower-TF data.
- Entries: Not a strategy — signal-only. Long signal: first confirmed close > or_h + break_buffer_atr*ATR after the OR window closes, within session (lines 209-212). Short mirror: close < or_l - buffer (211, 213). One-shot per side per day by default (61, 212-213). Secondary state: retest of the broken OR level within retest_tol_atr*ATR, either extreme-inside-band (default) or range-intersect variant (224-227, input at 66).
- Exits: None. No exits, stops, or targets — the script provides location context (OR, IB, session VWAP+SD, prior-day levels, gap in daily-ATR units, day-type heuristic) and break/retest events for a human or downstream system.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing of any kind; indicator has no order engine.
- Alerts: Four alertcondition()s (lines 307-310): breaks fire off confirmed-close conditions; retest alerts edge-detect the latch (retest_x and not retest_x[1]) so they fire exactly once. Parallel alert() calls for breaks (311-320) with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close and optional JSON payload (input line 75). Duplicate-fire risk: a user who arms both the alertcondition and has the alert() path active receives two notifications per break; also same-bar break+retest can produce a break alert and a retest alertcondition on the same close.
- State: All state is var and reset on sess_new (lines 136-144, 170-173, 185-187, 202-207): OR/IB accumulators (or_h/or_l/ib_h/ib_l, or_done/ib_done), day range, manual session VWAP sums (cum_v/cum_pv/cum_pv2 with SD via E[x^2]-E[x]^2, variance floored at 0 at line 179), VWAP-side counters (isconfirmed-gated), and the ORB state machine (break_dir, long_fired/short_fired one-shot latches per side per day controlled by one_shot_breaks at line 61, retest_long/retest_short latches). break_dir records only the FIRST break direction (lines 216-217, 220-221); the opposite side can still fire its break signal but never gets retest tracking (224/226 require break_dir to match). Warm-up: ATR(14) on chart TF (line 103) and daily ATR(14) (line 117) need history; nz() at 209/223 masks na ATR as zero buffer/tolerance. day_type requires side_n >= 6 confirmed bars (line 233). No arrays/matrices/varip.
- Translation feasibility: High. The entire decision core is faithfully translatable to intraday OHLCV Python: session windowing from timestamps, OR/IB accumulation over the first N minutes, cumulative session VWAP + SD from bar hlc3*volume, prior-day H/L/C and daily ATR from resampled dailies, gap in daily-ATR units, confirmed-close break with ATR buffer, one-shot latches, retest band logic (both variants), and the day-type heuristic. All signals are bar-close deterministic (no intrabar dependence). Only caveats: replicate the exchange-time session calendar (DST/half-days) and match TradingView's RMA-based ta.atr; the HUD/alerts/plots layer is untranslatable and irrelevant. Recommend fixing the same-bar retest ordering explicitly (require retest bar index > break bar index) in the translation.
- Repaint risks:
  - None material. Break signals (lines 210-213) require barstate.isconfirmed; historical bars are always confirmed, so historical/realtime parity holds.
  - HUD state and day_type_txt (lines 232-237, 281-302) evaluate intrabar close — display flicker only; the exported DAY_TYPE_EXPORT is latched on confirmed bars (239-241, 270).
  - Intrabar VWAP/SD (167-180) uses the forming bar's partial volume in realtime; committed bar-close values match historical. Same for bars_above/bars_below, which are isconfirmed-gated (188-190).
  - No varip, no label/box deletion-redraw of signals; plotshape marks (258-259) are driven by confirmed-close conditions and do not revise.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - Lines 117-118: request.security(syminfo.tickerid, "D", [high[1], low[1], close[1], ta.atr(14)[1]], gaps=barmerge.gaps_off, lookahead=barmerge.lookahead_on) — lookahead_on WITH the [1] offset is the standard non-repainting fixed-history pattern; NOT contaminating. All four series carry the offset.
  - No security call fetches a higher-TF unconfirmed current value. No other lookahead usage anywhere in the file.
  - gap_atr (line 122) uses day_open captured at session start (119-120) vs pd_close from the completed prior day — no future leak.
- Session/timezone:
  - Line 48: session string "0930-1600" interpreted in the exchange timezone via time(timeframe.period, sess) at line 108 — DST follows the exchange calendar; correct for US equities but wrong if the user leaves the default on a symbol whose exchange time differs.
  - Line 117: prior-day H/L/C come from the "D" bar of the data feed; on futures/crypto (ETH-inclusive dailies) these are NOT RTH prior-day levels, so pd_high/pd_low/gap_atr semantics diverge from the RTH session the rest of the script measures.
  - Lines 108-112: elapsed_min derived from wall-clock time - t0; half days / late opens shorten the session naturally, but a data gap over the OR window (missing first bars) makes the OR reflect only the bars present, with no detection.
  - Line 109: in_sess[1] is na on the very first chart bar, so the first session on the chart may not fire sess_new — one-session warm-up artifact only.
- TradingView-only:
  - table HUD (lines 279-302) — display only
  - plotshape break markers (258-259) and data-window export plots (264-274)
  - alert()/alertcondition() infrastructure (307-320)
  - input.session string parsing and time(timeframe.period, sess) session detection (48, 108) — must be re-implemented against an exchange calendar in Python
  - request.security daily feed semantics (117) — trivially replaced by resampled daily bars
- Known defects:
  - Lines 214-227: same-bar break-and-retest — the retest blocks run after the break latches in the same bar execution, so the breakout bar itself can set retest_long/retest_short if its low/high falls inside the tolerance band around the OR level; "break then price trades back" semantics are violated on that bar.
  - Lines 209, 223: nz(atr_val) converts na ATR (first 13 bars of chart history) to 0, silently disabling the break buffer and shrinking retest tolerance to 0 during warm-up instead of suppressing signals.
  - Lines 148-153 with line 114: OR window is quantized to chart-bar boundaries; a non-divisor TF (e.g. 2m chart, 5m OR) includes data through minute 6, and the HUD warning (286) only triggers when tf_min > or_minutes, not for non-divisor cases.
  - Line 109: in_sess[1] is na on the first chart bar, so sess_new can fail to fire for the first session in history — that day's OR/VWAP state never initializes (one-day warm-up artifact).
  - Lines 307-320: overlapping alertcondition and alert() paths for the same break events — duplicate notification risk if both are armed.
  - Line 121: "Chart ATR (v1.0)" gap option divides the overnight gap by a chart-TF ATR that itself contains the gap bar — acknowledged in header (lines 7-9) as the reason for the v1.1 default, but still selectable.

- Integrity: **`PASS_WITH_CONSTRAINTS`**
- Explanation: The decision logic itself is clean: the only security call (line 96) uses the safe close[1]+lookahead_on prior-value idiom; all state writes and signals are gated on barstate.isconfirmed (lines 114, 150-153, 167); alerts use alert.freq_once_per_bar_close (lines 229-236); no varip. Constraints preventing a clean PASS: (1) sigma off-by-one at line 119 — the ring has _days_eff+1 rows (line 109) but the read loop runs r = 0 to _days_eff - 1, so row index _days_eff is never read; whenever mrow < _days_eff (N of every N+1 days) only N-1 prior days are averaged, contradicting the v1.1 changelog claim (lines 10-11) of a true N-day average — a persistent band-width bias, though not lookahead. (2) Time-of-day alignment uses bar count day_b (line 105), not wall-clock time — half-days/halts/DST shifts corrupt the same-time-of-day premise. (3) When half_hour_checks is on with a misaligned TF, escapes silently never fire; the guard (line 142) only warns on the HUD, it does not gate or relax the check. (4) DAY_OPEN_EXPORT (line 194) is not warm-gated despite the header claim at line 14. It is a study with explicit, executable signal rules (escape_up/escape_dn) but no orders/stops/sizing.
- Timeframes: Intraday only (guard at line 142 flags non-intraday on HUD, line 204 shows "NOT INTRADAY — bands n/a"). Paper protocol assumes a TF that divides 30 minutes when half_hour_checks=true (lines 139-142); TFs like 7m never land on :00/:30 and are flagged MISALIGNED but signals silently never fire in that mode. Header (lines 18-20) states RTH charts are preferred: on extended-hours charts day_open anchors to the premarket open while the sigma model is RTH-premised.
- Symbols: Single chart symbol; equities/futures with a defined session. No hardcoded tickers. Sigma indexing is by bar-count-since-day-open (day_b, line 105), so symbols/days with halts, half-days, or missing bars misalign the "same time of day" premise.
- External data: One request.security call, line 96: request.security(syminfo.tickerid, "D", close[1], gaps=barmerge.gaps_off, lookahead=barmerge.lookahead_on) — same symbol, daily TF, [1] offset with lookahead_on. This is the standard non-repainting prior-day-close idiom; it returns the previous day's confirmed close and is NOT contaminating. No other tickers, no VIX, no lower-TF data.
- Entries: No entries (indicator). Signal rules: escape_up = confirmed close > UB where UB = day_open*(1+sigma)+max(prev_close-day_open,0); escape_dn = confirmed close < LB = day_open*(1-sigma)-max(day_open-prev_close,0) (lines 131-134, 150-155); sigma = mean of |close/day_open - 1| at the same bar-of-day over prior N sessions; optionally only on bars closing at HH:00/HH:30, optionally once per side per day.
- Exits: None. The header (line 30) notes the source paper trails with the bands and session VWAP (plotted, line 181), but no exit rule is coded — the study only maintains na_state (inside/above/below) per confirmed bar.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing, risk model, or capital logic of any kind.
- Alerts: Good discipline. alertcondition (lines 225-226) and alert() calls (lines 227-236) both key off escape_up/escape_dn, which require barstate.isconfirmed, and alert() uses alert.freq_once_per_bar_close — no intra-bar or duplicate fires. With one_shot_per_side=true, max one alert per side per day. Optional JSON payload (default off) with sigma/ub/lb/occupancy/time fields; the human/JSON split is display-layer only. Minor: alertcondition and alert() duplicate each other if a user wires both.
- State: Persistent state: var matrix mv 46x500 float ring buffer (line 101), mrow ring pointer cycling mod _days_eff+1 (line 109), day_b bar-of-day counter, days_seen, na_state (-1/0/1), one-shot flags up_fired/dn_fired reset at new_day (lines 146-149), occupancy counters in_bars/tot_bars (lines 162-169). No varip. Warm-up: warmed = days_seen > _days_eff (line 126), i.e. N+1 day-change events (~15 sessions at default 14) — the first partial day counts toward days_seen, so effective warm-up can be one day optimistic if the chart starts mid-day. na propagation is well guarded (lines 113, 131-134, 150-152). One-shot machine: escape per side per day when one_shot_per_side=true (lines 154-159).
- Translation feasibility: High. The full decision core is faithfully translatable to intraday OHLCV Python: group bars by session, take day_open as the first bar's open, prev_close as the prior session's last close, compute sigma(t) as the mean of |close_t/day_open - 1| at the same intra-day slot over the prior N sessions (preferably keyed by wall-clock time rather than bar index, which would also fix the alignment defect), form the gap-adjusted bands, and emit escape signals on bar closes at :00/:30. Session VWAP is a simple cumulative sum given volume. The N-vs-N-1 averaging bug (line 119) must be resolved deliberately in translation — decide whether to replicate the bug for parity or fix it. Not translatable/not needed: HUD, exports, alert plumbing. Daily-only OHLCV is insufficient; intraday bars are required.
- Repaint risks:
  - None material. Bands (ub/lb, lines 133-134) can drift intra-bar on the realtime bar because abs_move uses live close (line 113), but no matrix write occurs until barstate.isconfirmed (line 114) and na_state/escape signals only change on confirmed closes (lines 150-159), so confirmed-bar history matches realtime confirmed behavior.
