  - HUD table (lines 201-220) redraws on barstate.islast only — display layer, not signal.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - Line 96: lookahead_on WITH close[1] offset on the daily TF — this is the standard safe prior-value idiom, not contamination; it yields yesterday's confirmed close on every intraday bar of today.
  - No other security calls; no future-bar indexing anywhere.
- Session/timezone:
  - Line 93: new_day via ta.change(time("D")) uses exchange calendar days; on ETH charts the day open anchors to premarket (header note, lines 18-20), diverging from the RTH-premised paper sigma.
  - Line 139: minute(time_close) uses the exchange timezone; the half-hour protocol assumes bars close on :00/:30 in that timezone — sessions opening at :15/:45 or DST-shifted sessions can break the mapping even on 5m/15m charts that pass the divisibility guard at line 142.
  - Line 105: day_b is a bar counter, not clock time — half-days, halts, or data gaps shift which historical sigma slot a given wall-clock time maps to, silently comparing e.g. 10:30 today against 11:00 on a gap-shortened prior day.
- TradingView-only:
  - ta.vwap (line 172) — session-anchored VWAP; needs volume data and TradingView's session anchoring definition to replicate.
  - table/HUD rendering (lines 201-220), plotshape/bgcolor/fill display layer — cosmetic only.
  - Data-window export plots (lines 189-196) — a TradingView-specific export mechanism.
  - timeframe.in_seconds / timeframe.isintraday guards (lines 141-142) — chart-context checks with no Python analogue needed.
  - request.security prior-day close (line 96) — trivially replaced by the prior session's close in Python.
- Known defects:
  - Line 119: off-by-one in sigma averaging — loop 'for r = 0 to _days_eff - 1' never reads ring row _days_eff, so whenever mrow < _days_eff the average covers only N-1 prior days, contradicting the v1.1 fix claim at lines 10-11; correct loop is r = 0 to _days_eff skipping r == mrow.
  - Line 194: DAY_OPEN_EXPORT plotted without the 'warmed ?' gate, contradicting the header claim (line 14) that exports are warm-gated; the on-chart plot at line 180 IS gated.
  - Line 142: alignment guard is HUD-only — with half_hour_checks=true on a misaligned TF (e.g. 7m), escape signals silently never fire; the guard does not disable or bypass the half-hour filter.
  - Line 105: day_b bar-index slotting means sigma compares different wall-clock times on days with missing bars, halts, or half-days — a semantic defect in the 'same time of day' claim, not a crash.
  - Line 93/95: on bar 0 ta.change(time("D")) is na so new_day is na (treated as false) and day_open stays na for the entire first partial day — benign due to na guards, but shifts warm-up by up to one day.

Squeeze/exhaustion logic uses the chart timeframe directly.

HUD frames active squeeze as a "HARD BLOCK — no entries" advisory for downstream gate consumers via SQZ_BLOCK_EXPORT (116).
- Exits: None — no exits, stops, or targets. Exhaustion flags are explicitly risk context, not exit signals.
- Sizing: None — no position sizing of any kind.
- Alerts: Five alertconditions (143-147): squeeze release up/down (already confirm-gated via line 79), exhaustion up/down edge-triggered and confirm-gated (145-146), squeeze-start edge-triggered and confirm-gated (147). Dynamic alert() for releases (148-152) at alert.freq_once_per_bar_close with optional JSON payload — no duplicate-fire risk since release itself requires isconfirmed. Alert discipline is sound; note alertcondition firing frequency ultimately depends on the user's alert dialog setting, but the conditions themselves are close-confirmed.

No varip, no arrays. Warm-up: max(sq_len=20 for stdev/ATR/linreg, 7 completed daily bars for NR7, RSI/EMA/ATR lengths); na-guards present (91-92, 102-105). One-shot edges via x and not x[1] in alertconditions (145-147).
- Translation feasibility: High. Entire decision core is faithfully translatable to bar-close OHLCV Python: SMA/stdev Bollinger vs ATR Keltner squeeze, consecutive-bar counters, linreg momentum (numpy polyfit over close - avg(midrange, basis)), RSI/EMA/ATR exhaustion composite, and NR7/inside-day from a properly aligned daily resample (use previous completed calendar/session days only, matching the [1]+ offsets). Only the HUD/plot/alert plumbing is TradingView-only. Note ta.stdev uses population stdev and ta.atr/ta.rsi use RMA smoothing — replicate exactly for parity.

- Look-ahead analysis:

### 08 · Gap & Overnight Risk Classifier v1.1

- File: `research/pine/08_gap_overnight_risk_classifier.pine` · SHA-256 `f93ac5167b52a8bc…` · 223 lines · Pine v6 · 5 inputs
- Classification: **indicator** · Family: **statistical_readout** · Direction: none · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`REQUIRES_REWRITE`**
- Explanation: The decision/measurement logic is semantically sound and non-repainting — the security call (lines 68-69) uses the canonical [1]-offset + lookahead_on prior-value idiom, day_open is latched at session start (lines 72-73), fills register only on barstate.isconfirmed (line 92), and per-bucket stats finalize only on completed days (lines 124-136). However the script cannot compile as written: lines 98-99 push into arrays btf_bkt/btf_val that are declared later at lines 106-107 (use-before-declare, "Undeclared identifier" — compile-blocking). Fix is trivial (hoist the two var array declarations above line 83), after which the script would merit NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR/PASS-as-indicator: it has no trade rules, which is by design, not a defect.
- Timeframes: Intended for intraday charts (bars-to-fill, "today" tracking); on a daily chart every bar is a new_day (line 71) and the tool degenerates to gap-at-open classification with same-bar fill detection. Daily context is pulled via request.security "D" regardless of chart TF (lines 68-69).
- Symbols: Any gapping instrument, implicitly equities/ETFs with session gaps. 24h instruments (crypto, most FX) produce near-zero gaps and the classifier is vacuous. Gap semantics depend on whether the chart includes extended hours: extended-hours charts anchor day_open to premarket open (acknowledged in tooltip, lines 21-22, 31).
- External data: One request.security call (lines 68-69): same symbol (syminfo.tickerid), "D" timeframe, tuple [close[1], high[1], low[1], ta.atr(14)[1]], gaps_off, lookahead_on. Higher-TF data only; no other tickers, no VIX, no lower-TF requests.
- Entries: None — no strategy calls, no entries. Classifies the overnight gap at session open into 4 classes by |gap|/dailyATR (lines 75-81: None <0.25, Small <0.75, Significant <1.5, SHOCK >=1.5) with direction and breakaway flag (open beyond prior day's high/low).
- Exits: None. The tracked event is "gap fill" — price touching prior daily close (line 93: low<=pd_close for up-gaps, high>=pd_close for down-gaps) on a confirmed bar; per-bucket fill rates, Wilson bands, and median bars-to-fill are accumulated for display.
- Sizing: Not applicable — no position sizing; script is a readout/classifier.
- Alerts: Two alertcondition (lines 214-215): "Shock gap at open" (new_day and class 3) and "Gap filled" (rising edge of filled). Plus dynamic alert() (lines 216-220) gated by barstate.isconfirmed and the filled rising edge, with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close — good confirmed-bar discipline, low duplicate-fire risk. Optional JSON webhook payload (line 218) toggled by input (default off). alertcondition at 215 uses filled[1] edge detection on var state, which is valid since filled only changes on confirmed bars.
- State: Heavy var state, no varip: day_open (72), filled/fill_bar/day_bars (84-86, reset on new_day at 87-90), bucket counters b_n/b_fill (103-104), unbounded bars-to-fill event arrays btf_bkt/btf_val (106-107, grow one entry per filled gap over full history), running sum sum_abs_ga/n_days (117-118), and a one-day-delay latch y_class/y_dir/y_filled/y_abs_ga (120-123) that finalizes yesterday on the first bar of a new day (124-136) — correct ordering: 'filled' is reset (line 88) before y_* finalization but y_filled was latched separately at line 137 on the prior day. Warm-up: needs ~15 daily bars for ATR(14)[1] before gap_atr is non-na; first chart day has na pd_close so no classification. Stats require many completed days to be meaningful (UI dims n<20). stt_merged one-shot guard (lines 182, 209-211).
- Translation feasibility: High. The entire measurement core is faithfully translatable to deterministic Python over intraday OHLCV plus a daily OHLCV series: prior-day close/high/low and ATR(14) from completed daily bars (exactly what the [1]+lookahead_on idiom returns), day_open from the first intraday bar of each session, gap classification thresholds, bar-close fill detection, per-bucket counts/fill rates/Wilson intervals/median bars-to-fill. The only care points: replicate the chart's session convention (RTH vs extended) when defining day boundaries, and note that intrabar fills register at bar close (matching the Pine convention). Tables, alerts, and colors are display-only and drop out. The use-before-declare bug must be fixed (or simply ignored) in translation — the intended semantics are unambiguous.

  - Fill detection gated on barstate.isconfirmed (line 92): on historical bars isconfirmed is true every bar, in realtime it fires only at bar close, so historical and realtime behavior match at bar-close granularity. An intrabar touch is only recognized at that bar's close — a deliberate, consistent convention, not repaint.

- Look-ahead analysis:

### 09 · Breadth & Internals Proxy v1.1

- File: `research/pine/09_breadth_internals_proxy.pine` · SHA-256 `f50fdbc217549c2d…` · 188 lines · Pine v6 · 16 inputs
- Classification: **indicator** · Family: **regime_detection** · Direction: none · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR`**
- Explanation: This is a separate-pane market-internals readout with no entry/exit rules, no strategy() declaration, and no position logic — the HUD itself says "gate input, never a signal" (line 174). NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR is the correct status and is not a defect. Its own computation discipline is clean: all six request.security calls (lines 75-80) are same-timeframe, lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_off, so there is no lookahead contamination; all persistent session state (cum_tick, tick_pos/neg, tick_ext_up/dn, add_pos/neg, sess_bars, lines 83-110) is accumulated only under barstate.isconfirmed (line 100), so historical and realtime values converge and nothing repaints once a bar closes. Realtime intrabar values of tick_c/add_c/voldd/score fluctuate before confirmation (normal for lookahead_off same-TF security), but alerts are confirm-gated (lines 178-181) and the accumulators ignore intrabar ticks. Known behavioral caveats, not integrity failures: on daily+ TFs the score is structurally 0 (lines 72/91/135); day boundary uses the chart symbol's "D" period, which can differ from the NYSE session on 24h symbols; and RSP/SPY/QQQ security calls lack ignore_invalid_symbol (lines 78-80) so a bad symbol input halts the study.
- Timeframes: Designed for intraday charts (5m-15m implied by "5T Pine Suite" and session accumulation). Runs on any timeframe, but on daily+ charts new_day (line 72, ta.change(time("D"))) is true on every bar, so all session accumulators (lines 91-99) reset each bar and sess_bars never exceeds 1; the sess_bars < 6 gate (line 135) then forces score = 0 permanently, so the trend-day score and its alerts are structurally dead on daily+. The group tooltip (line 46) warns of degradation but understates it: score is not merely "unreliable" on daily+, it is always 0.
- Symbols: Chart symbol is arbitrary (only used for syminfo.ticker in the alert payload, line 183, and for the "D" day boundary on line 72). Hard-wired defaults: USI:TICK, USI:ADD, USI:VOLD (NYSE internals, require a US-internals data plan; degrade to na via ignore_invalid_symbol = true, lines 75-77), AMEX:RSP, AMEX:SPY, NASDAQ:QQQ (lines 39-41). US-equity-market breadth semantics assumed throughout (TICK extreme = 800, line 45).
- External data: Six request.security calls, all to timeframe.period (chart TF, never higher/lower): tick_sym close (line 75), add_sym close (line 76), vold_sym close (line 77) — each with gaps_off, lookahead_off, ignore_invalid_symbol=true; broad_sym RSP (line 78), cap_sym SPY (line 79), risk_sym QQQ (line 80) — gaps_off, lookahead_off, no ignore_invalid_symbol. Plus an input.source regime_src (line 43) intended to be wired to another suite script's REGIME_EXPORT plot.
- Entries: None. No trade entries. The nearest thing to a signal is the trend-day classification: dir = sign of (tick_side + add_side + vold_side) (lines 127-128) and a 0-100 heuristic score (lines 129-135) built from ADD persistence (>=70% -> +30, >=60% -> +15), TICK persistence (>=65% -> +25, >=55% -> +12), VOLD agreement (+20), QQQ/SPY slope agreement (+15), and 3+ TICK-extreme cluster (+10); day_state = "TREND UP/DOWN" when score >= threshold (default 70), else "RANGE / MIXED" (line 136). Explicitly labeled a gate/context input, not a signal.
- Exits: None — no exits, stops, or targets. Not a strategy.
- Sizing: None — no position sizing of any kind.
- Alerts: Three alertcondition calls, all AND-ed with barstate.isconfirmed (lines 178-180): trend-score threshold cross (rising-edge via score[1] < threshold, line 177), 3+ upside TICK extremes, 3+ downside TICK extremes (edge-triggered via [1] comparisons). Edge-triggering plus confirm-gating gives good duplicate-fire discipline; each can fire at most once per session per direction because the counters only rise within a day. Separately, a dynamic alert() (lines 181-185) fires on the same confirmed trend_cross with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close, emitting either human-readable text or an optional JSON webhook payload (use_json_alerts, line 49). Minor: alertcondition messages are static strings, so the JSON option applies only to the alert() path.
- State: Eight var accumulators (lines 83-90): cum_tick, tick_pos/neg, tick_ext_up/dn, add_pos/neg, sess_bars. Reset on new_day (lines 91-99), incremented only under barstate.isconfirmed (lines 100-110) with na guards on tick_c and add_c. Warm-up: score forced to 0.0 until sess_bars >= 6 (line 135); br_slope/rr_slope (lines 123-124) are na for the first ratio_slope_len bars (default 30) of chart history. No arrays/matrices, no varip, no one-shot latches. var table hud (line 157) is display state only. na propagation is handled explicitly everywhere (division guards lines 113/115/121/122; zero-data side votes return 0, lines 116-118 — the documented v1.1 fix).
- Translation feasibility: High for the core. Everything decision-relevant operates on confirmed-bar closes of seven series (chart symbol for day boundary; TICK, ADD, VOLD, RSP, SPY, QQQ closes at the chart TF), so a Python replica over intraday OHLCV bars reproduces it exactly: per-day reset, cumulative TICK, sign-persistence counts, extreme-cluster counts, RSP/SPY and QQQ/SPY ratio slopes over N bars, the additive score with the sess_bars>=6 gate, and the vote-based direction. Requirements: intraday TICK/ADD/VOLD feeds (not free in most data stacks) aligned to the chart-bar grid with the same gaps_off forward-fill-none semantics (na bars skipped, matching lines 102-110), and a day-boundary definition matching the chart symbol's exchange calendar. Untranslatable subset: regime_src source-input alignment readout, HUD, alerts. On daily bars the translation is trivial but pointless — score is structurally 0 (see timeframe notes).
- Repaint risks:
  - Realtime display values tick_c/add_c/vold_c (lines 75-77) and derived score/day_state (lines 129-136) fluctuate intrabar before bar confirmation — standard lookahead_off behavior; they settle at close and match historical recalculation. Persistent accumulators are isconfirmed-gated (line 100) and do not repaint.
  - The HUD table (lines 157-174) redraws on barstate.islast each tick — display-only, no signal revision implied.
  - No varip, no label/box deletion-redraw of signals, no unconfirmed-bar state mutation.

- Session/timezone:
  - Line 72: new_day = ta.change(time("D")) != 0 keys the session reset to the CHART symbol's daily period, not the internals symbols' NYSE session. On 24h/overnight symbols (futures, crypto) the 'day' includes bars where TICK/ADD are na (guarded, lines 102-110) but sess_bars still increments, diluting the sess_bars>=6 warm-up meaning and persistence denominators are unaffected only because na bars are skipped.
  - Daily-period boundaries follow the exchange calendar of the chart symbol; DST transitions can shift the session start on symbols whose day is defined in a non-US timezone, changing which bars fall in a 'day'.
  - No explicit session strings or time(session) windows — no half-day/holiday handling; NYSE internals simply read na outside their trading hours.
- TradingView-only:

The tooltip (line 46) calls this 'unreliable' but it is actually dead, which could mislead a user relying on the warning text.

  - Line 139-140: regime validity uses exact float equality (regime_src == -1.0/0.0/1.0). Correct for plot-wired int exports, but any source with rounding (e.g. a scaled or averaged export) silently reads n/a; math.round on line 140 is dead code given the equality guard.

  - Line 118: vold_side uses only the current bar's VOLD sign rather than any session-persistent measure, so one bar's VOLD flip can move dir and up to 20 score points intraday — by design but a fragility worth noting for translation parity tests.
  - No compile-blocking defects found: no use-before-declare, division guards present (lines 113, 115, 121, 122), ternaries are well-formed.

Stop moves to breakeven after T1 (position-shrink heuristic, lines 433-435), Chandelier trail (22-bar, 3x ATR) ratchets the runner once profit >= trail_after_r R (lines 437-440); one shared run_stop protects all remaining legs. Overriding regime exit: strategy.close on confirmed bar when regime flips against the position, optionally on neutral (lines 450-455). Within its own scope the decision logic is clean: the single security call (line 77) uses the safe [1]+lookahead_on idiom; all flag and scoring logic is gated on barstate.isconfirmed (lines 100, 105-106, 127), so flags do not repaint and alerts fire once per closed bar (alert.freq_once_per_bar_close, line 207). The v1.1 changelog fixes (session-rollover expiry lines 130-138, fail-before-win ordering lines 141-154, fail level snapshotted at flag time lines 158/164, na-guarded VWAP touch lines 145/152) are all correctly implemented as described. Remaining issues are minor measurement quirks (see known_defects), none of which repaint or leak future data. Not PASS solely because PASS is reserved here relative to executable decision logic; as a study, its logic is sound.
- Timeframes: Designed for intraday charts: session VWAP is rebuilt each daily rollover (line 80 ta.change(time("D"))) and the outcome horizon is measured in chart bars (default 30, line 42), which only makes sense below the daily timeframe. The daily regime filter via request.security("D", ...) (line 77) assumes the chart TF is <= D; on a weekly/monthly chart the "D" security call would be a lower timeframe and the regime read becomes meaningless. On a daily chart itself, every bar is a "new day" so every pending episode expires unscored immediately (lines 130-138) — the scoring engine degenerates.
- Symbols: Requires a symbol with volume (VWAP accumulators lines 85-91 use nz(volume); a zero-volume symbol leaves cv=0 and svwap=na forever, permanently suppressing flags via the nz(stretch) gate on lines 105-106 — silent no-op, not a crash). Works on equities/futures/crypto; "session" is really the exchange's daily bar boundary, so futures session VWAP includes the overnight/Globex portion of the trading day.
- External data: One request.security call, line 77: same symbol (syminfo.tickerid), timeframe "D", expression close[1] > ta.ema(close,20)[1], gaps_off, lookahead_on. This is the canonical non-repainting prior-completed-daily-bar idiom: lookahead_on combined with the [1] offset on BOTH series inside the expression returns yesterday's confirmed close-vs-EMA relationship on every intraday bar, identically on historical and realtime bars. Not contaminating. No other tickers, no VIX, no lower-TF requests.
- Entries: No entries — study flags only. Long flag (line 105): confirmed bar, side armed, RSI(close,2) <= threshold (default 10), stretch <= -2.0 session-VWAP sigma, and (optionally) prior daily close above prior daily EMA20. Short flag (line 106) is the exact mirror (RSI2 >= 90, stretch >= +2 sigma, daily regime down). One-shot per RSI-50 excursion.
- Exits: No exits — instead an outcome scorer per flag: episode WINS if high (long) / low (short) touches session VWAP within horizon_bars (default 30) before price moves fail_atr*ATR (default 1.5x ATR14, snapshotted at flag close, lines 158/164) adversely or the horizon times out (FAIL); pending episodes at the daily rollover expire unscored (removed from n, counted in exp, lines 130-138). Fail/timeout is evaluated before the win check each bar (conservative tie-break, lines 141-154).
- Sizing: None — no position sizing, no strategy.* calls, no risk functions beyond the ATR-based episode fail threshold used purely for scoring.
- Alerts: Two static alertcondition()s (lines 200-201) on the confirmed-bar flags. Additionally a dynamic alert() (lines 202-207) with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close; since long_flag/short_flag already require barstate.isconfirmed, duplicate-fire risk is nil. Optional JSON payload (input line 48, default OFF) embeds symbol/tf/side/rsi2/stretch/time; syminfo.ticker is interpolated unescaped (line 205) — harmless for normal tickers, malformed JSON only for exotic ticker strings containing quotes. A user with both alertcondition and alert() enabled would receive two notifications per flag — configuration overlap, not a script bug.
- State: Three interacting persistent machines, all var (no varip, no arrays): (1) VWAP accumulators cv/cpv/cpv2 (lines 81-91), reset on new_day; (2) one-shot arming flags armed_long/armed_short (lines 98-110) — a flag disarms its side until RSI-2 recrosses 50, preventing repeated flags within one oversold excursion; (3) episode scorers pl_*/ps_* (lines 113-166) — at most ONE pending episode per side (line 155/161: 'and not pl_on' silently drops a new flag that fires while an episode is pending, so flag-count exports can exceed l_n+l_exp+wins). Ordering within the confirmed-bar block is deliberate and correct: expiry (rollover) -> fail/timeout -> win -> new-episode start, so a flag bar itself is never scored and same-bar win/fail collisions score as fails. Warm-up: RSI-2 needs 2 bars, ATR needs atr_len bars, daily EMA20 needs ~20 daily bars (security returns na until then — na==1.0 is false, so daily_up is false during warm-up, biasing early flags short-side when the regime filter is on); stretch is na until cv>0 and nz() maps it to 0, suppressing flags — safe.
- Translation feasibility: Fully translatable to deterministic intraday OHLCV Python — this is one of the cleanest scripts in the suite for translation. Everything decision-relevant is bar-close arithmetic on OHLCV: RSI-2, ATR, cumulative session VWAP + variance from hlc3*volume, prior-day close-vs-EMA20 regime (resample to daily, shift(1)), the arming state machine, and the episode scorer with its fail-first ordering and rollover expiry. The only care points: (a) reproduce the exchange trading-day boundary for the VWAP reset (Globex vs RTH vs UTC), (b) match ta.rsi/ta.atr RMA-based warm-up seeding, (c) reproduce the nz(atr_val) warm-up quirk (line 158) or fix it deliberately. The HUD, plots, and alerts are presentation-only and droppable. The Wilson lower bound (lines 71-73) is one line of scipy-free math.
- Repaint risks:
  - None material. Flags (lines 105-106) and all scoring mutations (line 127 block) require barstate.isconfirmed, so nothing computed intra-bar is ever revised.
  - Line 77 security call uses expr[1] with lookahead_on — the standard non-repainting pattern; historical and realtime bars agree.
  - HUD table (lines 186-198) redraws only on barstate.islast and displays already-confirmed state; cosmetic only.
  - No varip anywhere. plotshape marks (lines 174-175) are driven by confirmed-bar flags and do not vanish or move.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - Line 77: lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on WITH [1] offsets on close and ta.ema(close,20) inside the expression — this is the safe prior-daily-value idiom, NOT contamination; it returns the last completed daily bar's regime on both historical and realtime bars.
  - No other security calls; the outcome scorer looks only backward (episode state advanced one bar at a time on confirmed bars, lines 141-154), so hit-rate statistics contain no future leakage.
- Session/timezone:
  - Line 80: new_day = ta.change(time("D")) != 0 keys the session VWAP reset and episode expiry to the exchange's daily bar boundary. For futures this includes the overnight session (VWAP anchored at Globex open, not RTH open); for 24/7 crypto it is the exchange's UTC day. DST shifts move the wall-clock reset time but track the exchange calendar, so behavior is consistent per-venue.
  - No hardcoded session strings or timezone literals; nothing breaks on DST, but 'session VWAP' semantics differ across asset classes and will not match an RTH-anchored VWAP on futures.
  - Line 205 JSON alert embeds raw time (ms epoch) — timezone-neutral, fine.
- TradingView-only:
  - table HUD (lines 186-198) — display only
  - plotshape flag marks and data-window export plots (lines 173-184) — display/export only
  - alertcondition/alert() delivery (lines 200-207)
  - request.security daily regime feed (line 77) — replaced trivially in Python by resampling to daily and shifting one day
  - time("D") session-boundary semantics (line 80) — must be replicated with the venue's trading-day calendar, not calendar UTC midnight, for futures
- Known defects:
  - Line 158/164: fail level uses nz(atr_val) — during ATR warm-up (first atr_len bars) the fail level equals the flag close itself, so the episode fails on the next bar's low<=close almost surely; a handful of warm-up episodes are near-guaranteed FAILs. Should skip flag scoring (or the flag) when na(atr_val).
  - Lines 105-106 vs 155/161: a flag that fires while an episode of the same side is still pending disarms the side (lines 107-110) and is exported via MR_*_FLAG_EXPORT (lines 179-180) but is never scored ('and not pl_on'); exported flag counts can exceed l_n + expiries, a silent bookkeeping discrepancy between exports and HUD stats.
  - Line 77: while daily EMA20 is warming up (~first 20 daily bars) the security expression is na, na==1.0 evaluates false, so daily_up=false — with require_daily_trend on, early history permits only SHORT flags; a small directional warm-up bias rather than suppression of both sides.
  - Line 190: HUD status ternary shows only 'LONG PENDING' when both a long and short episode are pending simultaneously — display-only, stats unaffected.
  - Line 205: syminfo.ticker interpolated into the JSON payload without escaping; malformed JSON possible only for tickers containing double quotes (rare).
  - Daily-chart degeneracy: on TF >= D every bar sets new_day (line 80), so every episode expires unscored on its first evaluation bar — the scorer silently never accumulates n; no guard or runtime.error warns the user.
- Related scripts:
  - 21 and 36-38 (Kalman/regime MR lineage sharing the f_wilson_lo self-scoring pattern found in 25 of the suite's scripts)
  - 05, 06, 14, 37, 38 (share the manual session-VWAP/svwap construction)
  - 35 (statistical edge validation consumes the same Wilson-lower-bound methodology)
  - 12 (expectancy journal — companion measurement module)

- Exits: None — no positions exist. Analogous "consumption" logic: pools consumed on any breach (v1.1 fix, lines 185-210); FVGs deleted on Touch/50%/Full mitigation (lines 245-262); OBs deleted on close through the far edge (lines 290-299).
- Sizing: None — not a strategy; no sizing, pyramiding, commission, or slippage concepts.
- Alerts: Five alertcondition() calls (lines 331-335), all driven by variables that are only true on barstate.isconfirmed bars — confirmed-bar discipline is inherent, no intrabar duplicate-fire risk. One dynamic alert() (lines 336-341) for sweeps with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close and optional JSON payload (input default OFF, line 74); JSON null-guards sweep_lvl_hit (line 339). sweep_lvl_hit records only the last pool touched when multiple pools breach in one bar (loop overwrites, lines 195/207) — alert payload ambiguity, not a correctness issue.
- State: struct_state (var int, line 117) is a 3-state machine (0 unset / 1 bull / -1 bear) with one-shot BOS/CHoCH transitions per confirmed bar. Pivot memory: last_sh/prev_sh/last_sl/prev_sl (lines 106-109). Capped arrays throughout: labels (60, line 124), liquidity pools + lines (liq_keep, lines 171/178), FVG boxes (fvg_keep), OB boxes (ob_keep). last_sweep_dir/last_sweep_bar (lines 164-165) remember only the single most recent sweep — an opposite-side sweep overwrites it, so the confluence check (lines 227-229) can only pair against the latest sweep. Warm-up: needs 2*piv_len+1 bars for first pivot, two same-side pivots for a pool, atr_len bars for ATR; nz(atr_val) guards early-na ATR (lines 167, 174, 236, 281). In-place array removal during descending-index loops (lines 188, 200, 246, 255, 291, 296) is index-safe.
- Translation feasibility: High. The entire event engine — pivot detection (with the 2*piv_len+1 confirmation lag), structure state machine, equal-level pool creation/consumption/sweep, FVG creation/mitigation, order blocks with optional ATR displacement gate, sweep→structure confluence — is pure confirmed-bar OHLCV arithmetic and translates faithfully to Python one-bar-at-a-time. The FVG/OB state must be re-homed from box objects to plain records (the boxes are both display and store). Killzones need a timezone-aware session calendar and a decision about which timezone is intended (exchange vs. NY/London). HUD, labels, and alerts are display-layer and drop out. ta.atr must match TradingView's RMA-smoothed ATR.
- Repaint risks:

- Session/timezone:

  - Lines 131-155: with struct_state == 0, a single wide-range confirmed bar that closes above last_sh in the first branch (setting state=1) can then satisfy the CHoCH-down branch at line 146 if it also closed below last_sl — bos_up and choch_dn both true on one bar. Rare (requires close simultaneously > last_sh and < last_sl, impossible unless last_sh < last_sl, which CAN occur after alternating pivots) — edge case, worth an assertion in translation.

One cross-symbol request.security to bond_sym (default TVC:US10Y) at "D" with close[1] + lookahead_on (line 84). One same-symbol request.security to "D" for ta.highest(high,252)[1] + lookahead_on (line 99). Fundamentals are late-arriving and subject to vendor revision — the header explicitly discloses this (lines 12-15) — so historical fundamental series are not point-in-time stable even though they are Pine-mechanically non-repainting.

Two consequences: (1) two consecutive quarters reporting the identical ROE value collapse into one entry, so 'N reports ago' (line 95) can actually be more than N calendar quarters back; (2) the count depends on how much chart history TradingView loads, so the same symbol on different chart depths yields different roe_delta availability. roe_then/roe_delta stay na until roe_n > roe_lookback_qtrs (lines 95-96) — na propagates cleanly to the HUD (line 128 shows a 'need N reports' notice). - File: `research/pine/16_pullback_to_value_playbook.pine` · SHA-256 `018733bb1ff57fef…` · 578 lines · Pine v6 · 52 inputs

- Explanation: The decision chain is genuinely non-repainting and lookahead-safe as written: the single request.security call (lines 192-193) is same-timeframe with lookahead_off — the safe pattern for chart-TF cross-symbol data (the [1]-offset idiom is only needed for higher-TF pulls, which this script does not do). The regime state machine commits only under barstate.isconfirmed (lines 175-176), fib pivots use ta.pivothigh/pivotlow with 8-bar right confirmation committed under barstate.isconfirmed (lines 227-249), the structural shelf excludes the current bar via low[1]/high[1] (lines 279-280), and both entry gates require barstate.isconfirmed (lines 310-311) with calc_on_every_tick=false and process_orders_on_close=false (lines 33-35), giving faithful historical/realtime parity with next-bar-open fills. Constraints preventing a clean PASS: (1) qty-split edge defect at lines 339-347/371 — when qty_step is large relative to min_qty, f_round_qty(_q/3) can floor leg-1 quantity to 0 while _q passes the entry gate, producing a qty=0 strategy.entry("L1V") and exits referencing a nonexistent leg (safe at defaults step=1/min=1, unsafe for configurable crypto/fractional setups); (2) the protective stop placed at signal close (_stop0, lines 358/372) is cancelled and replaced one bar later by leg_stop rebuilt from actual average fill (lines 413-414, 431-438), so realized initial risk differs from sized risk on gap fills — risk_pct sizing (line 335) is computed against signal-bar close, not fill; (3) T1 detection is a proxy — t1_done fires when entry L1V is simply no longer open (lines 416-417), so any leg-1 exit (not only the T1 limit) arms the breakeven move; (4) same-bar stop-vs-limit resolution across the three legs depends on TradingView's broker emulator intrabar path assumption, which is not reproducible from OHLCV alone. None of these contaminate the signal logic itself; they bound the fidelity of the execution layer.
- Timeframes: Timeframe-agnostic by construction (no session strings, no timeframe branching), but defaults are daily-swing calibrated: 200-bar Mansfield MA (line 65), 100-bar EMA regime filter (line 61), $1M average dollar-volume floor (line 107), 20-bar regime lookback with sqrt-of-window vol annualization proxy (lines 155-157). On intraday charts the SPY benchmark security call still uses chart TF (timeframe.period, line 192), so it degrades gracefully but the RS statistic loses its Mansfield weekly/daily meaning.
- Symbols: US equities/ETFs implied: benchmark default AMEX:SPY (line 64), dollar-volume liquidity floor assumes share-based volume (line 296), commission 0.03% + 2 ticks slippage models equity trading (lines 29-31). Requires a symbol with real volume: bars with na/zero volume contribute nothing to the AVWAP accumulator (lines 213-217), so quote-only symbols (indices, some FX feeds) would produce a permanently na AVWAP and no trades. Shorts disabled by default with explicit borrow/locate caveat (lines 51-52).
- External data: One request.security call, lines 192-193: request.security(bench_sym, timeframe.period, close, gaps=barmerge.gaps_off, lookahead=barmerge.lookahead_off). Same-timeframe cross-symbol fetch of benchmark close (default AMEX:SPY) for the Mansfield RS ratio. No higher/lower TF requests, no other tickers.
- Entries: Confirmed-bar confluence entry, next-bar-open market fill in three simultaneous legs. Long (lines 308, 310): regime==1 (vol-normalized window-return z > 0.85 with hysteresis, close > EMA100, 2-bar confirmation) AND Mansfield RS > 0 vs SPY (line 198, defaulted on) AND close inside the flip-anchored AVWAP lower band [avwap-1σ, avwap] (line 223) AND (default AND-mode) inside the active up-leg's 0.382-0.618 fib pocket (lines 253-257, leg must be ≥3 ATR and pivot-confirmed) AND a defense candle (close>open or lower wick ≥50% of range, line 276) AND no-chase RSI ≥ 35 with structural stop distance ≤ 8% (lines 292). Plus protections: no equity-DD halt, cooldown elapsed, dollar-volume floor, flat position. Short is the mirror (regime -1, RS<0, upper band, down-leg pocket, rejection candle, RSI ≤ 65) and is OFF by default (line 51). Entry places three legs L1V/L2V/L3V split in thirds with immediate protective stops at signal-close ± stop distance (lines 356-401).
- Exits: Layered, per-leg. On the first in-position bar the signal-close protective stops are cancelled and rebuilt from actual fill: leg_stop = avg ∓ plan_rdist (lines 413-414, 431-438). Leg 1 exits at T1 = avg ± 1R limit, leg 2 at T2 = avg ± 2R limit (t2_eff floor-guarded at line 408), both stopped at leg_stop (lines 443-446, 453-456). After leg 1 closes (proxy-detected via open-trade scan, lines 348-354, 416-417), leg_stop ratchets to breakeven ± offset (lines 420-422). Leg 3 is a runner on run_stop — a monotonic ratchet of leg_stop plus a chandelier trail (22-bar extreme ∓ 3×ATR) activated after +1R (lines 423-430, 447-448, 457-458). If splitting was infeasible, one leg takes limit=T2/stop=run_stop (lines 440-441, 450-451). Overlay exits on confirmed bars: adverse regime flip or adverse AVWAP cross closes all legs at market (lines 460-469). All stops/targets are strategy.exit stop/limit orders resolved by the broker emulator.
- Sizing: Fixed-fractional risk sizing: qty = (equity × risk_pct 1% / stop_distance), capped by notional cap_pct (100% of equity / close), floored to qty_step and rejected below min_qty (lines 334-338). Stop distance is structural (shelf low/high fused with the AVWAP band edge ± 0.25 ATR buffer, lines 279-288) with an ATR×2 fallback. Quantity split in thirds via f_split with remainder-merge logic (lines 339-347). Sized on signal-bar close but filled next-bar open, so gap fills change realized risk vs sized risk. Commission 0.03%, slippage 2 ticks, initial capital 100k declared (lines 28-31).
- Alerts: Good discipline. Two alertcondition hooks on can_long/can_short (lines 565-566), which embed barstate.isconfirmed — fire only on confirmed setup bars. Dynamic alert() (lines 567-573) offers JSON webhook or text payload, explicitly alert.freq_once_per_bar_close, and is gated by the same confirmed setup conditions including position_size==0 — no duplicate-fire or intrabar-flicker risk. Payload includes stop price, regime, Mansfield value, variant tag, and bar time. Alerts fire only when a new entry is actually eligible (suppressed while in a position); there are no exit alerts, so live-trading the alerts requires managing exits externally.
- State: Three persistent machines, all with sound na/warm-up discipline. (1) Regime: var confirmed_core (line 160) starts na; candidate computed with hysteresis (enter z 0.85 / hold z 0.55 + EMA agreement, lines 162-171); a confirm loop requires the candidate stable for reg_confirm bars (lines 172-174 — na history comparisons collapse cand_held to false, correctly blocking early commits); commit only on barstate.isconfirmed (175-176). regime stays na until first commit, and every downstream gate na-checks it, so no trades during warm-up. Flip tracking (lines 179-187) computes regime_flip before updating last_regime — correct ordering. (2) AVWAP: var accumulators av_pv/av_w/av_p2 reset on regime_anchor (lines 204-212), incremental sum of price*vol and price²*vol gives mean and variance; zero/na-volume bars are skipped (line 213). On the anchor bar itself av_sd=0, so the ±1σ band is zero-width and in_value effectively requires several bars to widen — an implicit deliberate cool-off after flips. Cross-anchor quirk: reclaim_dn/up (lines 460-461) compare avwap vs avwap[1] across an anchor reset, mixing two different anchor series on the flip bar — cosmetic-to-minor since regime-flip exit fires that bar anyway. (3) Trade plan: var plan_rdist/plan_q1..q3/legs_placed/t1_done/t2_done/leg_stop/run_stop (lines 316-325), reset on flat transition (326-332); one-shot per position (entries require position_size==0, line 310-311 — pyramiding=3 exists solely for the three simultaneous legs, not for adds). run_stop is a monotonic ratchet (lines 423-430). Cooldown and equity-peak trackers (lines 299-306) are simple and correct.
- Translation feasibility: High for the signal layer, medium for the execution layer. Fully faithful on daily OHLCV Python with an aligned SPY close series: log-return regime z with hysteresis + EMA + n-bar confirmation (lines 154-177), Mansfield RS (194-199), flip-anchored volume-weighted AVWAP mean/σ via the same incremental sums (204-222), pivot-confirmed fib pockets with 8-bar confirmation lag (227-258), candle triggers and RSI no-chase (272-293), structural stops (279-290), liquidity floor, cooldown, and equity-DD halt — all bar-close deterministic. The three-leg exit ladder translates only under explicit intrabar-order conventions (e.g., conservative stop-first assumption when a bar spans both stop and limit); next-bar-open fills, breakeven ratchet, and chandelier trail are straightforward. Not translatable: TradingView broker-emulator tie-breaking, order-state introspection semantics, and all HUD/alert plumbing. A single-exit or bracket-order simplification would make the Python backtest fully deterministic at modest fidelity cost.
- Repaint risks:
  - None material in the decision path. request.security (lines 192-193) is same-TF, lookahead_off — bench_close on the realtime bar is the benchmark's developing close, but all order decisions are gated by barstate.isconfirmed (lines 310-311, 462, 466), so realtime signals use only confirmed values, matching historical behavior.
  - AVWAP accumulators (lines 213-217) mutate on every execution, but with calc_on_every_tick=false the strategy executes once per realtime bar at close, and Pine's rollback commits var state only on confirmed bars — no intrabar accumulation drift.
  - No varip anywhere. Labels/boxes are not used; the two tables (lines 485-546) redraw only on barstate.islast and are display-only.
  - Fib pivot legs confirm piv_right=8 bars after the extreme (lines 236-249) — this is lag, not repaint: once a leg is set it never revises retroactively.
  - plotshape entry marks (lines 479-480) are driven by can_long/can_short which include barstate.isconfirmed, so they cannot appear then vanish intrabar on historical replay, though on the live bar they only appear at bar close.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - Lines 192-193: lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_off with a same-timeframe symbol — no future data access; the expr[1]+lookahead_on idiom is unnecessary here because no higher timeframe is requested. NOT contaminating.
  - Lines 227-228 / 236-249: pivot-based fib legs use bar_index - piv_right bookkeeping only after the pivot is fully confirmed — the leg becomes tradable information 8 bars after the swing extreme, which is causally correct (no peeking).
  - Lines 279-280: shelf uses low[1]/high[1] windows — explicitly excludes the forming bar. NOT contaminating.
  - Lines 501-506: OOS table splits closed trades by exit_time against a fixed input timestamp — display-only, no feedback into orders.
  - No timestamp arithmetic, no future-bar indexing, no negative offsets anywhere.
- Session/timezone:
  - No session strings, no time()-window gating, no timezone arguments — no DST sensitivity in the script's own logic.
  - Cross-symbol alignment risk (lines 192-193): with gaps=barmerge.gaps_off, if the chart symbol trades hours/days when AMEX:SPY does not (crypto, futures, non-US equities), bench_close repeats its last known value, silently flattening the Mansfield RS statistic (lines 194-199) over the misaligned span. On US-equity daily charts this is a non-issue; elsewhere it biases rs_long_ok/rs_short_ok.
  - oos_start input (line 55) is a fixed +0000 timestamp compared against exit_time — display-only.
- TradingView-only:
  - Broker emulator intrabar path assumption for same-bar stop-vs-limit resolution across three simultaneous strategy.exit legs (lines 439-458) — not reconstructible from OHLCV.
  - strategy.opentrades.entry_id scan for T1 detection (lines 348-354, 416-419) — engine-internal order-state introspection.
  - strategy.equity / strategy.closedtrades introspection for sizing, DD halt, and the validation table (lines 300, 335-336, 487-516).
  - Two table.new HUDs and validation panel (lines 485-546) — display only.
  - plotshape/bgcolor/fill visuals (lines 474-480), data-window export plots (lines 551-560).
  - alert()/alertcondition webhook plumbing (lines 565-573).
  - request.security benchmark fetch with barmerge gap handling (lines 192-193) — replaced in Python by an aligned benchmark series.
- Known defects:
  - Lines 339-347 + 371: qty-split floor defect — with qty_step large relative to min_qty (e.g., step=10, min_qty=1, _q=10), _can is true but f_round_qty(_q/3) floors leg-1 to 0, so strategy.entry('L1V', qty=0) is submitted while exits reference L1V; the remainder-merge at 344-346 only repairs leg 3, never leg 1/2. Safe at defaults (step=min=1); latent for fractional/crypto configs.

- File: `research/pine/17_hmm_regime_probability_filter.pine` · SHA-256 `bcfce3eb014eb4cd…` · 334 lines · Pine v6 · 20 inputs
- Classification: **indicator** · Family: **regime_detection** · Direction: none · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR`**
- Explanation: This is a regime-probability filter/readout with no entry, exit, stop, target, or sizing rule — declared indicator() at line 55, no strategy.* calls anywhere. That is by design, not a defect: it exists to emit HMM_REGIME_EXPORT (-1/0/+1, line 277) and probability exports (lines 274-283) for consumption by the suite's BULL+/BEAR+ engines. Its own computation is disciplined: the forward recursion (lines 184-201), warm-up counter (148-149), empirical transition counting (161-167), flip detection (211-216), and all self-measurement accumulators (239-261) run only under barstate.isconfirmed, so historical and realtime behavior match and intrabar values hold the last confirmed state exactly as the header claims (lines 37-39). No request.security, no varip, no lookahead. Alerts are confirmed-bar gated plus alert.freq_once_per_bar_close (319-329). Residual nits are cosmetic/mild (see known_defects), the most substantive being that in Empirical transition mode the current bar's own shadow transition is counted (161-167) before the same bar's prediction step uses the matrix (190-192) — still strictly causal (no future data), just a same-bar ordering inconsistency with the textbook HMM formulation.
- Timeframes: Timeframe-agnostic; no timeframe.* branching except timeframe.period echoed in alert payloads (lines 325, 327). Defaults (obs_lookback=20, warm_bars=100, p_stay=0.92) are tuned in bar units, so regime dwell semantics scale with whatever chart TF the user runs.
- Symbols: Symbol-agnostic. Uses only chart close for log returns (lines 142-143); requires close>0 (guarded), so fine for equities/futures/crypto. syminfo.ticker used for alert text only. No exchange/session assumptions.
- External data: None. Zero request.security() calls, no other tickers, no higher/lower TF data. The only external hook is input.source regime_src (line 88), an optional user-wired plot from another indicator on the same chart, strictly validated to {-1,0,+1} (line 222) and used for an agreement readout only — it gates nothing.
- Entries: None. No trade entries. The closest thing to a signal is the committed-state flip event: state_flip when the confirmed hmm_state (argmax probability clearing commit_pct, default 60%, lines 203-206) changes from the prior bar's committed state (211-214).
- Exits: None. No exits, stops, or targets. Dropping below the commit threshold produces the UNCOMMITTED state / flip_to_uncommitted event (214), which a consuming engine could treat as a regime-gate release, but this script executes nothing.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing of any kind.
- Alerts: Three alertcondition()s (319-321) for flips to BULL/BEAR/UNCOMMITTED, each driven by state_flip which embeds barstate.isconfirmed (211) — fires once at bar close, no intrabar duplicates. Dynamic alert() (322-329) is doubly protected: state_flip gating plus alert.freq_once_per_bar_close, with JSON payload optional (default OFF, line 98). Payload includes null-guarded obs_z (325). Clean confirmed-bar discipline; duplicate-fire risk negligible.
- State: Persistent state is extensive and all confirmed-bar gated: z_seen warm-up counter (147-149, warm_bars default 100 valid obs_z bars required; exports emit na before warm per line 208); shadow_state plus 9-cell tr_counts and 3-cell tr_rows int arrays for the empirical transition matrix (156-167), counted on every chart regardless of mode but only read when trans_mode=Empirical; the three forward-probability vars initialized to uniform 1/3 (181-183) with a 1e-10 floor and renormalization (194-201) preventing degeneracy/na poisoning; prev_committed_state one-bar-lagged commit state (210-216); agreement counters (223-227); per-side next-bar hit counters and dwell accumulators (235-261). na propagation is handled: obs_z na bars skip the recursion entirely (holding prior belief), f_gauss returns na on bad sigma and the recursion checks all three emissions (188). Empirical-mode results are path-dependent on full chart history depth — same settings on different history lengths give different matrices (disclosed in tooltip, line 81).

The entire decision core is deterministic, causal, close-only math on OHLCV: log window return / sqrt(N)-scaled stdev z-score (142-145; note ta.stdev is population stdev — match ddof=0 in numpy), Gaussian emissions (136-137), sticky-prior or Laplace-counted transition matrix (170-176), 3-state forward recursion with floor and renormalization (184-201), commit threshold and flip events, plus all Wilson-bound self-measurement. ### 21 · Kalman Filter MR Detector v1.0

- File: `research/pine/21_kalman_filter_mr_detector.pine` · SHA-256 `d3e061b17fd8d2bf…` · 333 lines · Pine v6 · 16 inputs
- Classification: **indicator** · Family: **mean_reversion** · Direction: bidirectional · Confidence: high

- Explanation: This is a study with no strategy.* calls, no entries, exits, stops, or sizing — flags are repeatedly declared "study events, never signals" (lines 14, 80, 317, 322-323). That is by design, not a defect. Within its own scope the decision logic is exemplary: the Kalman recursion, flag state machine, and episode scoring all advance only inside barstate.isconfirmed blocks (lines 151, 193, 206, 230); the innovation z uses the PRIOR level and predictive variance computed before the update (lines 160-166), so no self-lookahead in the trigger; there is no request.security, no varip, and alerts fire once per bar close (lines 327, 329). One caveat keeps this from being an unqualified clean bill: the self-scoring WIN check (lines 244, 251) compares the current bar's high/low against kf_level AFTER that bar's update (KF block lines 151-169 runs before the scoring block at 230), so the touch target already incorporates the current close — a mild within-bar information bias in the hit-rate statistics only, not in flag generation. Deterministic and faithfully reproducible, but the scored hit rates are slightly flattering versus a strictly causal touch-of-prior-level test.
- Timeframes: Timeframe-agnostic; ATR^2-scaled Q/R (lines 71-75, 152-154) is explicitly designed to transfer across timeframes. Intraday-specific behavior only in the session-rollover expiry branch gated by timeframe.isintraday (line 231). horizon_bars=30 default assumes reasonably granular bars; on daily charts an episode can span ~6 weeks.
- Symbols: Symbol-agnostic by design (ATR^2 noise scaling, lines 72-74). No syminfo gating; syminfo.ticker used only in alert text (lines 327-329). Works on any symbol with valid close and ATR; bars with na close or ATR<=0 are skipped entirely (line 151), pausing the filter rather than corrupting it.
- External data: None. Zero request.security() calls. The only external wiring is regime_src = input.source(close, ...) (line 83) — a chart-layer source hookup to another indicator's REGIME_EXPORT plot, validated to strict {-1,0,+1} (line 177) and used for display/alert text only, explicitly not gating anything (lines 82-84, 179-183).
- Entries: No trade entries. Flag events only: LONG-side stretch flag when confirmed, warmed, armed, and innovation z <= -flag_z (default 2.0, line 197); SHORT-side when z >= +flag_z (line 201). One shot per episode; re-arms once |z| < 0.5 (lines 194-196). z = (close - prior_level)/sqrt(P_pred + R) with Q = q_scale*ATR^2, R = r_scale*ATR^2 (lines 152-163).
- Exits: No trade exits. Episode self-scoring only: WIN = high/low touches the (moving) Kalman level within horizon_bars (default 30); FAIL = adverse move beyond fail_atr (1.5) x ATR-snapshotted-at-flag, or horizon expiry; fail/timeout checked BEFORE the win touch so same-bar collisions score conservatively (lines 240-253); intraday pending episodes expire unscored at session rollover (lines 231-239).
- Sizing: None. No position sizing anywhere; pure study.
- Alerts: alertcondition per side with static text (lines 322-323) plus dynamic alert() calls at alert.freq_once_per_bar_close (lines 324-329), JSON payload optional (default OFF, line 96). Flags can only be true on the confirmed tick, and freq_once_per_bar_close caps firing — no duplicate-fire or intrabar-alert risk. JSON string is hand-built (line 327); variant_tag/ticker containing quotes could break JSON validity, a theoretical webhook edge case.
- State: Persistent state: var floats kf_level/kf_P and counters kf_bars, innov_c, innov_z_c, band_sd_c (lines 143-148); one-shot flag arms armed_long/armed_short re-armed when |z| < 0.5 (lines 188-204); state_code 0/1/2/3 (lines 205-207); per-side episode trackers pl_*/ps_* with tallies l_n/l_w/l_exp, s_n/s_w/s_exp (lines 218-229). No varip, no arrays/matrices. Warm-up: state initializes level=close, P=R on first valid bar (lines 155-158); flags gated until kf_bars >= warm_bars (default 50, lines 76, 171, 197, 201). Bars with na close or ATR<=0 are skipped, so kf_bars counts only stepped bars. Only one pending episode per side at a time — a new flag while an episode is pending is counted (l_n increments at line 258 only when not pl_on... actually a flag cannot fire while un-rearmed, but the one-episode-per-side design means overlapping episodes are impossible by construction). Expired intraday episodes decrement n and increment exp (lines 232-239), so timeouts/fails are implicit losses (n - w - exp).
- Translation feasibility: Fully translatable. The entire decision core is closed-form on OHLCV: ATR (Wilder RMA, length 14), the 1-D Kalman predict/update recursion (lines 151-169), the one-shot flag machine with 0.5 re-arm (lines 188-204), and the episode scoring engine including snapshot fail levels, fail-before-win ordering, and session-rollover expiry (lines 230-263) — all deterministic given bars plus an exchange session calendar for the intraday rollover. Wilson lower bounds (lines 129-131) are trivial. Only presentation (HUD, shapes, alerts) and the input.source regime wiring are TradingView-only. Two translation notes: replicate the exact execution order (KF update before scoring within a bar) to reproduce the scored stats including their within-bar touch bias, or deliberately fix that bias by testing touch against the prior bar's level; and replicate Pine's skip-bar behavior when ATR<=0 or close is na.

- Look-ahead analysis:

  - Scoring-only within-bar bias: lines 244/251 test high/low against kf_level already updated with the SAME bar's close (KF block 151-169 executes before scoring block 230-263). The touch target partially 'knows' the bar being tested. Affects hit-rate/Wilson stats only, never flag emission.

Flag generation is unaffected.

### 25 · Walk-Forward Regime Stability Analyzer v1.0

- File: `research/pine/25_walk_forward_regime_stability.pine` · SHA-256 `8d8c01f046a7df17…` · 284 lines · Pine v6 · 14 inputs

The one structural caveat is the chunk assignment at line 149: ck_now = min(segments-1, floor(bar_index*segments/last_bar_index)). last_bar_index is the index of the last loaded bar, so every historical bar's chunk index depends on total history length — the historical series of ck_now, cur_fr (line 214), and the WFS_* exports (lines 223-226) is a full-sample statistic, not a causal time series, and re-chunks whenever new bars load. The header discloses this honestly (lines 26-28, 143-147). Within a loaded session the counts never revise. As a chart-time study this is fine; treating the exported plots as historical signals would be lookahead-contaminated, hence a study verdict rather than PASS-as-signal.
- Timeframes: Timeframe-agnostic; operates on whatever chart timeframe is loaded. Chunk statistics are per-bar, so meaning scales with bar size (flips/100 bars). z_lookback default 20 bars implies at least ~20-bar warm-up plus min_chunk_bars=20 per chunk before any chunk counts.
- Symbols: Symbol-agnostic; requires close > 0 for the log-return fallback classifier (lines 131-132), so any conventional price series works. No exchange or asset-class assumptions.
- External data: No request.security() calls anywhere. Optional external input.source link (line 69, regime_src) intended to be wired to another suite script's REGIME_EXPORT plot; strictly validated to exact -1/0/+1 at line 137 with fallback to internal z classifier. No other tickers or timeframes.
- Entries: None. No strategy calls, no signal intended for execution; the study explicitly states nothing here gates or signals anything (line 28).
- Exits: None.
- Sizing: None. No sizing, pyramiding, stops, targets, commission, or slippage — not a strategy.
- Alerts: alertcondition at line 274 plus alert() at lines 275-280 for verdict changes, both effectively confirmed-bar (verdict_change is only set true inside the barstate.isconfirmed block, line 210, and alert() uses alert.freq_once_per_bar_close). Duplicate-fire risk low; note the first-ever verdict fires as a 'change' due to the -9 sentinel (lines 167, 210), and both alertcondition and alert() firing for the same event means a user who configures both gets two notifications. Optional JSON payload (line 277) is well-formed; cv null-guarded.
- State: Fixed-size persistent arrays of length 8 (lines 152-159) hold per-chunk flips/bars/bull/bear/dwell/|z| accumulators; only indices 0..segments-1 are touched, so segments max 8 is safe. var scalars prev_reg_s (sentinel -9), cur_dwell, flips_total, dwell totals (lines 160-164). verdict_i uses sentinel -9 (line 167) so the first computed verdict fires verdict_change (line 210). Warm-up: fallback classifier needs z_lookback bars of returns (obs_z na until then, regime_ready false, bars not counted); each chunk needs >= min_chunk_bars counted bars and >= 2 usable chunks before a verdict exists. na propagation handled: obs_z guarded (lines 131-134), all rate divisions guarded by count checks (lines 200, 208, 214-215, 255-258).

Default tod_max_bars=400 sized for 390 one-minute RTH bars (line 71); works on any intraday TF with proportionally fewer slots.
- Symbols: Single chart symbol; assumes a volume feed (nz(volume), line 163 — symbols without volume silently produce RVOL of 0/na). Day boundary from time("D") (line 133), so session/date semantics follow the exchange calendar. Equity RTH framing in commentary; works on crypto with midnight-UTC-style day boundaries too.
- External data: None. Zero request.security() calls. Optional external regime via input.source (line 77) — a same-chart plot link from another indicator, strictly validated to exact -1/0/+1 (line 146) with internal z-score fallback (lines 140-148). No other tickers, no higher/lower TF fetches.
- Entries: None. Closest thing to a signal is the "hot" condition: elevated time-of-day RVOL (>= 1.5x default) while regime is directional (line 281), explicitly labeled selection context, never an entry.
- Exits: None. No exits, stops, or targets — not a strategy.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing of any kind.
- Alerts: Two channels, both confirmed-bar disciplined: alertcondition on hot_new AND barstate.isconfirmed (line 283), and alert() calls gated by barstate.isconfirmed with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close (lines 284-289). hot_new is an onset edge (hot_now and not hot_now[1], line 282), so no duplicate fires while the condition persists. Optional JSON payload (default OFF, line 92) with symbol/tf/rvol/regime/variant/time. Note the alertcondition condition includes barstate.isconfirmed, which on historical bars is always true — standard, but if the user configures the alertcondition at once-per-bar frequency it could fire on an intrabar hot_new flicker only at the moment isconfirmed becomes true; effectively still close-tick. Low risk.
- State: Persistent state: var matrix 60x500 of cum volume (line 158), var tod_row/day_b/chart_cum (lines 159-161), and eight var int follow-through counters (lines 191-198). Row rotation modulo _days_eff with per-row na clear on new day (lines 166-169). Warm gate: tod_n >= 5 prior days with data at this slot (line 182). Counters accumulate over full chart history with no decay — statistics blend all loaded history. Known warm-up nuance: the first (usually partial) day writes into row 0 and IS included in later averages (see defects). na propagation handled: rvol_tod na until warm/intraday, tilt verdicts na ("thin") below min_side_n (lines 228-231), Wilson returns na for n<=0 (line 126). No varip.
- Translation feasibility: HIGH — the entire decision core is faithfully translatable from intraday OHLCV bars: cumulative volume per day, per-slot rolling average over prior N days with current-day exclusion, elevation threshold, log-return z-score regime fallback, per-regime elevated-vs-baseline next-bar continuation counts with Wilson lower bounds and the tilt-margin verdict. All arithmetic is closed-form on confirmed bars. The only untranslatable pieces are display/alert plumbing and the external regime link (trivially replaced by passing another module's regime series). Positional slot indexing must be reproduced exactly (bar count from day start, not wall clock) to match Pine, including its half-day misalignment quirk.
- Repaint risks:

- Session/timezone:
  - Day boundary via ta.change(time("D")) != 0 (lines 132-133): follows the symbol's exchange calendar/timezone, so DST transitions do not break day detection, but they can shift which wall-clock time a given bar-of-day slot represents if the chart includes extended hours misaligned across DST.
  - Bar-of-day index (day_b, line 162) is positional, not clock-based: half-days, late opens, halts, or missing bars shift every subsequent slot's alignment for that day, quietly blending different times of day into one matrix column. No half-day exclusion exists.

  - Data-window export plots (lines 243-247) — replaced by return values in Python.
- Known defects:

The 5-day warm gate (line 182) softens but does not remove this.
  - Row-clear scope: on new day only columns 0.._bars_eff-1 of the new row are cleared (lines 168-169). If a user raises tod_max_bars mid-session after having run with a smaller value, stale cells beyond the old range... in practice inputs recompile the whole script, so this is unreachable; noted as a latent assumption only.
  - HUD warming counter (line 256) prints str.tostring(math.min(tod_n, 5)) + "/5" — cosmetic clamp is correct, but tod_n is the count at the CURRENT slot; early in a session with irregular history the displayed warm progress can jump between slots bar to bar. Cosmetic only.
  - Silent zero-volume symbols: nz(volume) (line 163) turns na volume into 0, making tod_sum 0 and rvol_tod na (guarded at line 181) — safe, but no HUD message distinguishes 'no volume feed' from 'warming'.
  - Wilson comparison asymmetry (lines 228-231): tilt compares the elevated side's Wilson LOWER bound against the baseline's RAW rate — the baseline gets no uncertainty treatment, so a noisy small-n baseline can suppress or grant tilt. The header does label the verdict a heuristic (line 47-48), so this is disclosed, not hidden.

Within its own stated scope the logic is non-repainting and lookahead-safe.

- Exits: Demo only: strategy.close on ta.crossunder(EMA9, EMA21) on confirmed bars (lines 56-57). No stops, no targets. The tester makes no exits.

Caveat: flips are only detectable in realtime since the verdict is computed only on barstate.islast.
- State: Extensive var persistent state (lines 121-147, 263): ~20 cached statistics plus rt_last_scanned as the scan-dirty flag (init -1, so first islast bar always scans, including the 0-trade case), rt_merged one-shot for table cell merges (lines 250-253), rt_prev_verdict for flip detection (line 263, init -1 so the first computed verdict never fires a spurious flip — both states must be known, line 264). No varip, no arrays/matrices. Warm-up: stats are na until the first bar where barstate.islast is true; slow demo EMA (21) needs normal EMA warm-up. na propagation is guarded throughout with explicit _n > 0 / not na() ternaries (lines 188-201).

The demo EMA-cross strategy is also trivially translatable (next-bar-open fills, 0.03% commission, slippage). TradingView-only and untranslatable-as-is: the table UI, alert plumbing, and the dependence on TradingView's broker emulator for the trade list — in Python the trade list must come from your own engine, which is the intended production use anyway.

Weights are properly na/zero-guarded (lines 137-149: vol1>0 checks and inv_sum>0 before division). The one soft edge is the input.source regime link (line 67): its correctness depends entirely on the external source being non-repainting — outside this script's control, though the strict {-1,0,+1} validation (line 157) fails safe to NEUTRAL.
- Timeframes: Chart-timeframe agnostic: all four request.security calls use timeframe.period (lines 128-131), so volatility and weights are computed on whatever TF the chart runs. vol_len default 20 bars (line 63) implies a warm-up of ~vol_len+1 bars per sleeve.
- Symbols: Defaults SPY/QQQ/IWM/GLD (lines 53-59) but fully user-configurable; ignore_invalid_symbol=true with na-guards (lines 137-140) makes it symbol-robust. Chart symbol itself only appears in alert payloads (syminfo.ticker, line 237). Mixed-exchange calendars can produce na bars on some sleeves; gaps_off carries last value forward, mildly staling that sleeve's vol.
- External data: Four request.security tuple calls (lines 128-131), each to a user-chosen symbol at the CHART timeframe (timeframe.period), lookahead_off, gaps_off, ignore_invalid_symbol=true, evaluating f_volpack() (close plus stdev of 1-bar log returns, lines 124-126). No higher/lower-TF requests. Optional input.source regime link (line 67) expects an external indicator's plotted {-1,0,+1} export — strictly validated at line 157.
- Entries: None. No entries — the script computes inverse-volatility percentage weights (w_i = (1/vol_i)/sum * 100, lines 141-149) over included, data-valid sleeves and displays them.
- Exits: None. No exits, stops, or targets of any kind.
- Sizing: Advisory only: effective exposure per sleeve = weight × gross_pct/100 (lines 165-168), where gross_pct is a user-input template (100/60/30 defaults, lines 69-74) selected by the optional regime link. Explicitly percent-only, no currency, no account awareness; comments state the suite risk layer outranks this panel.
- Alerts: alertcondition (line 234) plus optional alert() (lines 235-241, JSON or human text) fire when any sleeve weight moved more than shift_pts vs shift_bars ago. Both gated by barstate.isconfirmed via shift_event (line 233) and alert() uses alert.freq_once_per_bar_close — good confirmed-bar discipline, no duplicate-fire risk. Note the condition is a level (shift persists across bars), so consecutive-bar alerts can recur once per bar while the shift stays above threshold; it is not edge-triggered.
- State: Only persistent object is `var table hud` (line 192) — cosmetic. No varip, no arrays/matrices beyond a transient str.split (line 117), no one-shot state machines. Warm-up: each sleeve needs vol_len+1 bars before ta.stdev of log returns is valid; ok-flags (137-140) hold weights na until then, and the weight-shift alert additionally needs shift_bars (default 20) of valid weight history (line 226) — na history yields shift 0.0, correctly suppressing early alerts (line 227).

The entire decision core is trivially translatable to daily/intraday OHLCV Python: per-symbol log returns, rolling stdev(vol_len), inverse-vol weights renormalized over valid sleeves, regime-scaled gross template, and the weight-shift event (|w - w.shift(shift_bars)| > shift_pts on closed bars). Only nuances: replicate gaps_off forward-fill when aligning multi-symbol calendars, and supply the regime series {-1,0,+1} directly instead of the input.source link. HUD/plots/alert plumbing are TradingView-only but carry no logic.

Both use the [1]-offset + lookahead_on fixed-history idiom (non-repainting). percentrank is computed inside the daily security context then [1]-offset, so the ranking itself is over completed daily closes only. No lower-timeframe or chart-symbol data requests.

No entries — the script classifies the index-vol regime as LOW/NORMAL/ELEVATED/STRESS from the VIX 252d percentile (line 128) with a one-notch backwardation escalation (line 129), and counts historical stress-spike decay episodes. The only semantic caveats are honestly labeled: worst-bar loss is divided by CURRENT ATR, not event-time ATR (line 159, disclosed lines 29-30, 210), and skew/kurt use biased population estimators (n divisors, lines 156-157), acceptable and deterministic.
- Timeframes: Timeframe-agnostic; operates on the chart's own bars (log close-to-close returns). Window default 100 bars regardless of TF. Threshold defaults (excess kurt 1/3) are calibrated with daily equity returns in mind per comments, but nothing enforces a TF.

- Entries: None — no trade entries. Classifies tail state: FAT-TAILED (2) if excess kurtosis > kurt_fat (3.0) or skewness < skew_fat (-1.0); ELEVATED (1) if excess kurtosis > kurt_elev (1.0); else ORDINARY (0) (lines 162-163).
- Exits: None — no positions, no exits.
- Sizing: None — indicator only, no sizing.
- Alerts: alertcondition on state escalation (line 221) plus runtime alert() on the same esc_ev (lines 222-229) with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close; escalation-only (tr_state > prev_state, line 167), de-escalation silent; confirmed-bar gated so no intrabar duplicate fires. Dual alertcondition + alert() means a user who wires both could get two notifications for one event — by design, not a bug. Optional JSON payload (line 225).
- State: Two var float arrays (rets, chgs, lines 111-112) act as a ring buffer capped at `win` via push/shift (lines 116-118); note changing the `win` input mid-session only trims going forward. var floats m_mean/m_sd/m_skew/m_kurt/m_semir/m_worst_atr (lines 125-130) persist last confirmed values; na until warmed (n_obs >= win, line 120), so ~win+1 bars warm-up. prev_state (line 166) is a one-step memory for escalation-only alerting; first valid bar cannot fire (prev_state na). If _sd == 0 (flat window) moments retain stale prior values rather than going na (lines 153-158) — a deliberate hold, but worth knowing. No varip.
- Translation feasibility: Fully translatable core: on each confirmed bar append log(close/close[1]) and close-close[1] to a deque of maxlen=win; when full, compute population mean/sd, skew (m3/sd^3), excess kurtosis (m4/sd^4 - 3), downside semidev ratio, worst change / current Wilder ATR(14); apply the threshold state machine and escalation flag. All of this is exact from daily/intraday OHLCV closes in Python (pandas rolling or a deque). Only the ATR needs Wilder's RMA replication for exact match. HUD/tint/alert plumbing and the regime-link source input are display-layer and non-translatable/irrelevant.

Residual caveats are environmental, not logical: session/extended-hours dependence of the gap definition and chart-timeframe dependence of the N-bar window.

- Symbols: Any symbol with meaningful session gaps (equities/futures). Uses syminfo.tickerid for the daily security call, so extended-hours vs RTH chart setting changes both the daily close reference and the day-open anchor (tooltip at line 59 acknowledges this). 24/7 crypto would yield mostly "None"-class gaps.
- External data: One request.security call (lines 117-118): same symbol, "D" timeframe, tuple [close[1], ta.atr(atr_len)[1]] with lookahead_on — the standard non-repainting prior-completed-day idiom. Optional input.source regime link (line 62) expects an external indicator's REGIME_EXPORT plot (-1/0/+1), strictly validated at line 130.
- Entries: None. No strategy calls, no entry rules — the tool counts whether gap days (classed None/Small/Signif/Shock at 0.25/0.75/1.5 daily-ATR) are followed by a regime-label flip within N confirmed bars, versus an overlapping-window baseline.
- Exits: None. Watches "exit" by flip (resolve as hit) or expiry after watch_n bars (resolve as miss), lines 174-183 — bookkeeping only, not trade exits.
- Sizing: None — no position sizing, no strategy() declaration.
- Alerts: alertcondition (258) plus optional alert() with JSON or text payload (259-266), all keyed to shock_watch_ev which is only set inside a barstate.isconfirmed block (159-165) and fired at alert.freq_once_per_bar_close — confirmed-bar discipline, at most one fire per shock day-start bar; no duplicate-fire risk. JSON alerts default OFF (line 77).
- State: Persistent state: var day_open (120), var z_seen warm-up counter (135, requires warm_bars confirmed non-na obs_z values, default 60), var reg_state prior label (143), var known_streak (147), pending-watch parallel arrays pend_cls/pend_left (152-153) rebuilt each confirmed bar via keep-arrays (no mid-loop removal — correct pattern), cumulative per-class counters cls_n/cls_flip (154-155) and baseline base_n/base_hits (156-157). Watches opened before resolution in the same confirmed-bar block (161-165) so a flip on the gap bar itself counts, as documented. If the regime source goes na (invalid link), flip detection is suppressed but pending watches still age and can resolve as no-flip (174-183) — a mild censoring bias during link outages. Statistics accumulate from chart history start, so results depend on loaded bar count.

The entire counting logic is deterministic on confirmed OHLCV bars: prior-day close and ATR from a daily series (shifted by one day), gap classing, the internal z-classifier (log window return / scaled stdev of 1-bar log returns, lines 131-138), flip detection, per-class watch resolution, overlapping baseline, and Wilson lower bounds all translate faithfully to Python with intraday bars plus a daily frame and an exchange session calendar. Only the external-link mode and the HUD/alert layer are TradingView-specific. Care points: replicate the day-boundary/premarket-open anchor for the chosen data (RTH vs ETH), the warm-up gate (60 confirmed z observations), and the exact watch semantics (gap bar counts as bar one; a flip resolves ALL open watches simultaneously).
- Repaint risks:

- Session/timezone:

  - DST: time("D") day boundaries follow the exchange timezone, so DST transitions shift session start in UTC terms; gap classing itself is boundary-relative and robust, but any Python replication must use exchange-calendar day boundaries, not fixed UTC.

The HUD's honesty footer flags the baseline overlap but not this watch overlap.

  - Line 261: JSON gap_atr uses f_fmt which returns the string "n/a" (unquoted) if gap_atr were na — guarded by the ternary emitting null, so no actual malformation; noted as fragile if the guard is ever removed.

- Symbols: Symbol-agnostic by design (ATR scaling stated as the transfer mechanism, lines 72-75). No hardcoded tickers. syminfo.ticker used only in alert text (lines 356-358). The `time("D")` day-rollover (line 233) inherits the exchange calendar of whatever symbol is charted.
- External data: NONE. Zero request.security() calls anywhere in the file. The only "external" input is `regime_src = input.source(close, ...)` (line 85) — a same-chart source wiring to another indicator's REGIME_EXPORT plot via the TradingView source-input mechanism, validated to strict {-1,0,+1} at line 190 with automatic fallback to the internal classifier. No other tickers, no higher/lower timeframe fetches.
- Entries: No trade entries. Study events only: a LONG-side stretch flag fires on a confirmed, warmed bar when innovation z <= -flag_z (default -2.0) while armed; SHORT-side when z >= +flag_z (lines 207-218). One shot per episode; re-arms when |z| < 0.5. The regime in force (external strict {-1,0,+1} link or internal window-return z classifier, lines 183-191) is snapshotted at flag time to route the episode into one of six (regime x side) ledgers — flags themselves are never regime-gated (line 84 tooltip).
- Exits: No trade exits. Episode scoring only: WIN if price touches the current Kalman level within horizon_bars (default 30); FAIL if adverse move exceeds fail_atr x ATR-at-flag (default 1.5) or the horizon expires, with fail/timeout checked BEFORE the win each bar (lines 255-268); intraday pending episodes expire UNSCORED at session rollover (lines 246-254).
- Sizing: None. No position sizing, no strategy.* calls, no risk functions — decision-support indicator by explicit declaration (lines 40-41, 60).
- Alerts: Clean confirmed-bar discipline. Two alertcondition() hooks (lines 349-350) on flag_long/flag_short, which can only be true on confirmed bars (set inside barstate.isconfirmed, line 207), so they fire at bar close without intrabar flicker or revision. Dynamic alert() (lines 351-358) uses alert.freq_once_per_bar_close explicitly, with human-readable text by default and opt-in JSON payloads (use_json_alerts, default OFF, line 100) carrying symbol, timeframe, side, regime tag, regime source, z, level, variant tag, and epoch time. No duplicate-fire risk: flags are one-shot per episode.
