Kalman state: var kf_level, kf_P, kf_bars, innov_c, innov_z_c, band_sd_c (lines 148-153), initialized on first valid bar with level=close, P=R (lines 160-163); warm-up gate kf_bars >= warm_bars (default 50, line 176) blocks flags and band until converged. One-shot flag machine: var armed_long/armed_short with re-arm at |z| < 0.5 fixed (lines 146, 202-218); state_code 0-3 for HUD (219-221). Regime snapshot var last_flag_regime taken at flag time (lines 214, 218). Six-ledger scoring: three int arrays of size 6 (led_n/led_w/led_x, lines 234-236) plus two pending-episode blocks (pl_*/ps_*, lines 237-244) — at most one long and one short episode pending at a time. Session rollover decrements n and increments x (expire-unscored, lines 246-254). na propagation is guarded at every division. Bars with na ATR or atr_val <= 0 skip the filter entirely (line 156) — kf_stepped stays false and no flag can fire that bar (line 207), a subtle gap-handling behavior a translation must match.
- Translation feasibility: HIGH — essentially the entire decision core is faithfully translatable to bar-close OHLCV Python: the 1-D Kalman recursion with ATR^2-scaled Q/R (lines 156-174), Wilder ATR, innovation z, one-shot flag machine with fixed 0.5 re-arm (202-218), the internal regime classifier (log window return / stdev(log returns)*sqrt(lb), lines 183-187), and the six-ledger episode scorer with its conservative fail-first ordering, ATR-at-flag fail distance, and horizon expiry (245-280), plus the Wilson lower bound (133-135). Everything advances on confirmed bars, so a vectorized or loop-based daily/intraday replication is exact given the same OHLCV feed. Care points: (1) replicate ta.stdev's population-vs-sample convention and ta.atr's RMA seeding; (2) replicate the same-bar ordering where the touch bar's kf_level is the level AFTER updating on that bar's close; (3) skipped bars when ATR is na/zero must also skip the filter step; (4) the intraday session-rollover expiry needs the exchange calendar; (5) the external regime link cannot be translated as-is — feed the regime series directly. The script's own logic is clean: no request.security anywhere; all state advancement (vol, quantile projections, coverage tally) is gated on barstate.isconfirmed (line 166), so sigma_bar/q-levels/coverage never change intrabar; the coverage self-check at lines 167-173 reads q05_v[k_horizon]/q95_v[k_horizon] BEFORE reassigning the current bar's projection (lines 177-181), and since [k] indexing is unaffected by the current-bar := this correctly scores the band projected exactly k bars ago against today's confirmed close — no off-by-one, no lookahead. Forward fan lines/labels are deleted and redrawn only on barstate.islast (lines 196-209) and anchor to the last CONFIRMED bar's stored values (anchor_bar/anchor_close set inside the isconfirmed block, lines 182-183), so the drawing is display-layer repaint of forward projections, not signal revision. alert() calls use alert.freq_once_per_bar_close (lines 252, 254) and fire only when breach flags set on a confirmed bar. If forced to grade the analytics as if executable it would be PASS-quality; the correct enum for a no-trade-rule tool is NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR.
- Timeframes: Timeframe-agnostic. Bands are computed in per-bar units (sigma per bar, horizon k in bars), so any chart TF works. The only TF-aware code is f_bpy() (lines 127-132), which converts to bars-per-year for the HUD's annualized sigma display; intraday annualization assumes 252 sessions x user-set session hours (line 76, default 6.5) and is explicitly display-only — band math never uses it (comment lines 33-36, 77).
- Symbols: Any symbol with positive prices (close>0 guards at lines 137, 166). Lognormal/GBM geometry assumed; header explicitly discloses fat-tail miscalibration (lines 17-25). 24h markets require setting sess_hours=24 for correct HUD annualization only.
- External data: None. Zero request.security() calls; single-symbol, chart-timeframe only.
- Entries: None. No strategy calls, no entry rules. Nearest event logic: breach flags when a confirmed close lands above the 95% or below the 5% quantile projected k bars ago (lines 169-173), explicitly labeled "calibration event, not a signal".
- Exits: None. No exits, stops, or targets.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing of any kind.
- Alerts: Two alertcondition()s (lines 247-248) plus dynamic alert() (lines 249-254, human-readable or JSON per input, default human-readable) — all keyed to breach flags that can only be true on a confirmed bar (set inside barstate.isconfirmed block) and alert() uses alert.freq_once_per_bar_close, so no intrabar duplicate-fire risk. Note both alertcondition and alert() fire for the same event, so a user who arms both gets doubled notifications — a configuration caveat, not a bug.
- State: var floats q05_v..q95_v, anchor_bar, anchor_close (lines 155-161) hold the latest confirmed projection; var ints cov_n/cov_in (lines 162-163) accumulate the coverage tally monotonically over all history. breach_up/breach_dn are per-bar (non-var, reset each bar, lines 164-165). Warm-up: sigma_bar is na until ta.rma(vol_len=20) seeds; coverage scoring additionally needs k_horizon (20) more bars before q05_v[k] is non-na, so first coverage sample arrives ~40+ bars in; all na paths are guarded (lines 137, 139, 166-167, 184-186). Drift option uses ta.sma over drift_len=250 with nz() fallback (line 174), so enabling drift before warm-up silently degrades to zero drift — disclosed behavior.
- Translation feasibility: Very high — the analytic core is trivially and exactly translatable to Python on daily/intraday OHLCV closes: per-bar log returns, Wilder RMA of squared returns (alpha=1/vol_len) for sigma, closed-form quantile bands band(q)=close*exp(m*k + z_q*sigma*sqrt(k)) with m=-sig2/2 (or SMA drift), the k-bar-lag coverage tally, and the Wilson lower bound (line 123-125) are all pure deterministic math on confirmed closes. Only the drawing/HUD/alert plumbing is TradingView-only. Replication caveat: reproduce RMA's TradingView seeding (SMA seed) for bit-exact warm-up.
- Repaint risks:
  - Forward fan lines/labels are deleted and redrawn on every barstate.islast execution (lines 196-209). This is cosmetic re-anchoring of a forward projection from the last confirmed bar — the underlying quantile series (q05_v..q95_v) only update on confirmed bars, so no decision value repaints.
  - Plotted series q05_v etc. hold last-confirmed values intrabar (assignment gated at line 166), so plots are flat during a forming bar — non-repainting by construction.
  - No varip, no security, no unconfirmed-bar logic anywhere.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - None. No request.security() calls exist, so no lookahead argument to audit.
  - Coverage scoring (lines 167-173) uses q05_v[k_horizon]/q95_v[k_horizon] — a strictly backward reference to a projection stored k bars earlier; the projection made at bar t uses only data through bar t (sigma from ta.rma of past log returns, line 138). No future data touches any computation.
- Session/timezone:
  - No session strings or time() windows. Only session-adjacent input is sess_hours (line 76), used solely in f_bpy() (lines 127-132) for the HUD's annualized sigma display; a wrong value mislabels the HUD percentage but cannot affect bands, coverage, or alerts.
  - Coverage horizon k is in bars, so overnight/weekend gaps compress calendar time relative to the k-bar horizon on intraday charts — a modeling caveat, not a code bug.
- TradingView-only:
  - Forward fan drawing via line.new/label.new arrays (lines 194-209)
  - table-based HUD (lines 226-242)
  - alertcondition/alert infrastructure and format.mintick string formatting
  - display.data_window export plots (lines 217-221)
- Known defects:
  - No functional defects found. Minor observations: (1) line 240 evaluates sigma-derived and coverage strings with f_fmt na-guards — safe; (2) doubled-notification possibility if a user arms both alertcondition (247-248) and the runtime alert() (249-254) for the same breach event; (3) line 174: with use_drift ON, median uses raw mean log return without a -sigma^2/2 adjustment — internally consistent as a log-space quantile model, but the two drift modes are not on the same convention (disclosed as estimation noise in tooltip, line 74); (4) mu_hat (line 140) is computed on every bar including unconfirmed realtime ticks but is only consumed inside the isconfirmed block, so no repaint leak.
- Related scripts:
  - Header references the suite's Tail-Risk filter script (line 20) as the companion fat-tail lens; shares the suite-standard Wilson lower bound, variant-tag, HUD, and JSON-alert conventions common across the 5T Pine Suite catalog.
### 40 · Black-Scholes Regime-Adjusted Implied Edge Gauge v1.0

- File: `research/pine/40_black_scholes_implied_edge_gauge.pine` · SHA-256 `8bd5356cf6003195…` · 247 lines · Pine v6 · 16 inputs
- Classification: **indicator** · Family: **valuation_context** · Direction: none · Confidence: high
- Integrity: **`NON_EXECUTABLE_INDICATOR`**
- Explanation: This is a readout/calculator with no trade rules — that is by design, not a defect; the header (lines 5-19) states it plainly. There is no strategy() declaration, no entries/exits/sizing, and the tri-state gauge (line 171: ratio >= rich_cut -> +1, <= cheap_cut -> -1, else 0) is a labeled heuristic classification of user-typed IV vs chart realized vol, not a signal with an execution rule. The script's own logic is clean for what it is: zero request.security() calls, no varip, no lookahead of any kind; per-bar BSM arithmetic (lines 145-161) is a pure function of inputs and that bar's close; the persistent gauge state machine advances only under barstate.isconfirmed (lines 175-183), so alertcondition triggers (lines 234-236) and alert() calls (lines 237-242, freq_once_per_bar_close) are confirmed-bar disciplined with no duplicate-fire path. Ordinary intrabar plot mutability applies to the live pane line and the data-window exports (lines 196, 201-205) — standard, not contaminating. Minor nits only: BSG_GAUGE_EXPORT (line 205) exports the instantaneous gauge_now rather than the confirmed gauge_state, and the regime link uses strict float equality (line 190).
- Timeframes: Chart-timeframe agnostic. Annualization in f_bpy() (lines 135-140) branches on timeframe.isintraday/isdaily/isweekly/ismonthly: intraday uses 252 sessions x user-input session hours (sess_hours, line 81, default 6.5) divided by bar seconds; daily 252/multiplier, weekly 52/multiplier, monthly 12/multiplier, fallback 252. RV percentile fixed at 252 bars (line 169). Intraday RV accuracy depends on the user setting sess_hours correctly (e.g. 24 for crypto/FX); a wrong value scales RV and shifts the gauge.
- Symbols: Any symbol with a close series; no options chain data exists or is requested — IV, strike offset, DTE, rate, and yield are all typed inputs (lines 69-77). Strike is re-derived each bar as close x (1 + offset%) (line 147), i.e. a rolling constant-moneyness hypothetical, not a live contract. Equity-session defaults (252 sessions, 6.5 hours) bias toward US equities/indices unless reconfigured.
- External data: None. No request.security() calls anywhere (header claims chart-local by design, line 54, and the body confirms it). The only external hook is input.source regime_src (line 89), a user-wired plot from another indicator on the same chart/timeframe, validated to strict {-1,0,+1} (line 190) and used for display/alert text only — it never enters the arithmetic.
- Entries: None — no entry rules exist. The only classification logic is the IV/RV gauge (line 171): user-typed IV divided by annualized close-to-close realized vol; >= rich_cut (default 1.2) = IV RICH (+1), <= cheap_cut (default 0.8) = IV CHEAP (-1), else NEAR (0). Explicitly labeled a disclosed heuristic, not an edge (header lines 27-31 cite the variance risk premium as the reason IV RICH is the normal resting state).
- Exits: None — no exit, stop, or target logic. Breakeven (line 161) is the textbook expiry breakeven (strike +/- premium, financing ignored) shown on the HUD for education only.
- Sizing: None. No position sizing, risk functions, pyramiding, commission, or slippage — not a strategy.
- Alerts: Three alertcondition hooks (lines 234-236) on gauge transitions to RICH/CHEAP/NEAR; their driving bools are true only on the confirmed-bar tick (state machine gated at line 177), so even a user-selected once-per-bar frequency cannot fire intrabar. Programmatic alert() (lines 237-242) fires on the same confirmed-bar gauge_changed with alert.freq_once_per_bar_close — no duplicate-fire path. Payload is human-readable text by default or JSON when use_json_alerts is on (line 93, default OFF); JSON null-guards every na field (line 240). alertcondition message strings are static (cannot carry series values), the dynamic detail lives only in alert(). Minor cosmetic risk: f_fmt (lines 120-122) uses round(x*10^d)/10^d then str.tostring, which can occasionally emit float artifacts (e.g. 1.2000000000000002) into the JSON — still valid JSON numbers.
- State: One persistent variable: var int gauge_state = 0 (line 175), a tri-state (-1/0/+1) updated only when barstate.isconfirmed and iv_rv_ratio is not na (lines 177-180); gauge_changed is a per-bar bool driving both alertcondition flags (lines 181-183) and alert() emission (line 237). No arrays, matrices, or varip. Warm-up: one_bar_ret needs 1 prior bar (line 166), ta.stdev needs rv_len bars (line 167, default 20), ta.percentrank needs 252 further bars of non-na rv_ann_pct (line 169) — so RV percentile is na for roughly the first 273 bars; the gauge itself is valid after ~21 bars. na propagation is guarded throughout (lines 152-161, 166, 168, 170) and the state machine explicitly skips na ratios, so gauge_state initializes at 0 and cannot be poisoned during warm-up.
- Translation feasibility: Near-total. The entire computational core is a pure function of the close series plus scalar inputs: BSM pricer with dividend yield (lines 145-161) using the A&S 7.1.26 erf approximation (lines 126-133, replaceable by scipy.stats.norm.cdf, which is strictly more accurate), delta, expiry breakeven; close-to-close log-return stdev annualized by a configurable bars-per-year constant (lines 166-168); 252-bar percent rank (line 169); tri-state gauge with hysteresis-free cuts and confirmed-bar transition detection (lines 170-183). All of this translates exactly to daily/intraday OHLCV Python — confirmed-bar semantics map to computing on completed bars, which is the only mode a bar-based backtest has. One deliberate divergence to document: Pine's ta.stdev uses population (biased, /n) stdev, so Python must use ddof=0 to match. Not translatable / not applicable: HUD, alerts plumbing, input.source regime wiring (replace with a passed-in regime series), and there is nothing to backtest — it emits a context gauge, not trades.
- Repaint risks:
  - Line 196 and lines 201-205: plotted ratio and data-window exports update intrabar on the live bar and settle at close — standard plot behavior, not signal revision; the alert-driving state machine is confirmed-bar only (line 177).
  - Line 205: BSG_GAUGE_EXPORT plots gauge_now (instantaneous, line 171) rather than the confirmed gauge_state (line 175); a downstream script reading this export intrabar sees an unconfirmed value that can flip before bar close. Consumers should apply their own [1]/confirmed-bar discipline.
  - Lines 210-229: HUD table redrawn only on barstate.islast — cosmetic last-bar refresh, no signal implication.
  - No varip anywhere; no unconfirmed-bar decision logic.
- Look-ahead analysis:
  - None. Zero request.security() calls in the file, so no lookahead argument exists to misuse. All series references are current-bar close or close[1] (lines 147, 166); ta.stdev/ta.percentrank (lines 167, 169) are trailing-window functions. No future-bar access of any kind.
- Session/timezone:
  - No session strings, no time() windows, no timezone arguments — no DST sensitivity in code paths.
  - Lines 135-140 (f_bpy): intraday annualization assumes bars uniformly tile 252 sessions of sess_hours each; half-days, holidays, extended-hours charts, or a wrong sess_hours input (e.g. leaving 6.5 on a 24h market) systematically mis-scales annualized RV and therefore the IV/RV gauge. Disclosed as an approximation on the HUD (line 223) but still the single largest calibration sensitivity.
  - Line 146: T = DTE/365 calendar-day convention, disclosed (header line 36); differs from trading-day conventions real desks use.
- TradingView-only:
  - table-based HUD (lines 210-229) — display only
  - data-window export plots as a cross-script wiring convention (lines 201-205)
  - input.source regime link consuming another indicator's plot on the same chart (line 89, lines 189-191)
  - alertcondition/alert() infrastructure (lines 234-242)
  - timeframe.* introspection for annualization branching (lines 135-140) — replaced by explicit bars-per-year config in Python
  - format.mintick price formatting (lines 219, 221, 240)
- Known defects:
  - Line 205: BSG_GAUGE_EXPORT exports gauge_now (instantaneous, line 171) instead of the confirmed gauge_state (line 175) — internal alerts are confirmed-bar but downstream consumers of this export get an intrabar-mutable value; inconsistent discipline between the script's own state and what it publishes.
  - Line 190: strict float equality (_rv_link == -1.0 etc.) validating the regime source — works for exports that plot exact integer constants, but any upstream that computes its regime value arithmetically could fail validation on representation error; a tolerance compare (math.abs(_rv_link - 1.0) < 1e-9) would be robust.
  - Lines 135-140: intraday bars-per-year assumes every session is exactly sess_hours and every bar is full-length; partial sessions and holidays bias annualized RV. Disclosed (header lines 32-34, HUD line 223) but uncompensated.
